Regulatory action	Approval
Approved dosage (if applicable)	6 to less than 12 years old (less than 40 kg): Three tablets of vanzacaftor 4 mg/tezacaftor 20 mg/deutivacaftor 50mg once daily

L51: ## I. Executive Summary
L52: ## 1. Overview
L53: ## 1.1. Summary of Regulatory Action
L54: The Applicant, Vertex Pharmaceuticals, submitted a new drug application (NDA) for the novel oral fixed-dose combination of vanzacaftor (VNZ), tezacaftor (TEZ), and deutivacaftor (D-IVA) for the treatment of cystic fibrosis (CF) in patients aged 6 years and older who have at least one *F508del* (F) variant1 or another response variant in the cystic fibrosis transmembrane conductance regulator (*CFTR*) gene. The NDA was reviewed by a multidisciplinary team. Each team recommends approval for patients with CF aged 6 years and older with at least one *F508del* variant or another responsive variant in the *CFTR* gene, and the signatory authority for this application concurs with these recommendations.
L55: To support the NDA for VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA, the Applicant submitted the results from two phase 3, randomized, double-blind, noninferiority trials: VX20-121-102 (Trial 102) and VX20-121-103 (Trial 103). Both trials demonstrated the noninferiority of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA to elexacaftor/tezacaftor/ivacaftor (ELX/TEZ/IVA), the current standard of care in this population, as measured by pulmonary function in subjects aged 12 years and older with CF. The primary endpoint was percent predicted forced expiratory volume in 1 second (ppFEV1) through 24 weeks, an established endpoint for this indication. Additional secondary endpoints supported these results. The efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA is extrapolated to patients aged 6 through 11 years of age from the adolescent and adult populations of Trials 102 and 103. Additional support comes from exploratory efficacy endpoint data from the open-label trial, Trial VX21-121-105 (Trial 105), in the 6- to 11-year-old population.
L56: The available safety database was adequate for review and demonstrated that VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA is safe for its intended use as labeled. The data support that the risks of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA are comparable to those of ELX/TEZ/IVA and include the potential risk for drug-induced liver injury and liver failure. The risk of hepatotoxicity will be managed by the inclusion of a Boxed Warning in labeling and increased monitoring through an enhanced pharmacovigilance strategy that will attach to this approval. The remaining observed adverse reactions were like those for ELX/TEZ/IVA and will be addressed through labeling and routine pharmacovigilance.
L57: The NDA also included appropriate preapproval nonclinical and clinical pharmacology studies. A nonclinical postmarketing requirement and clinical pharmacology drug-drug interaction postmarketing requirement will attach to this approval.
L58: 1 Throughout this review the term “variant” will be used per the most recent American College of Genetics and Genomics (ACMG) standards (Richards et al. 2015; den Dunnen et al. 2016). Note that in the United States Prescribing Information (USPI) for VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA and Applicant materials, the term “mutation” is used. In the context of this review, these terms are synonymous.
L59: The review team concludes that the benefit of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA, based on noninferiority to ELX/TEZ/IVA as measured by pulmonary function, outweighs the risks when VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA is used as recommended in the approved labeling. There are no outstanding issues from any review discipline. The signatory concurs with the content of the various discipline assessments and their recommendations for approval. The Agency and Applicant have agreed upon the final labeling language. The submitted clinical program is adequate to support the efficacy and safety of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA for the treatment of CF in patients 6 years of age and older with a copy of the *F508del* variant or another responsive *CFTR* variant. VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA will offer an alternative, once-daily therapy for many patients with CF and will be a new therapy for some patients with certain *CFTR* variants. The action for this application will be Approval.
L60: ## 1.2. Conclusions on Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L61: Substantial evidence of effectiveness (SEE) was established with two or more adequate and well-controlled clinical investigations.
L62: To support the proposed indication for VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA, the Applicant completed two phase 3 efficacy and safety trials, Trials 102 and 103. The trials demonstrated SEE for VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA in subjects with CF with a responsive *CFTR* variant. The determination of efficacy is based on demonstration of the noninferiority of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA to ELX/TEZ/IVA on the primary endpoint of the absolute change from baseline in the ppFEV1 through 24 weeks. Evaluation of in vitro data from Ussing chamber electrophysiology experiments demonstrated greater activity for the triple drug combination, VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA, compared to the mono-components and dual-components.
L63: The proposed indication includes 303 variants in the *CFTR* gene, representing over 90% of the U.S. CF population. To support the inclusion of the individual *CFTR* variants, the Applicant submitted a combination of clinical data, in vitro data, and a proposal for the extrapolation of safety and efficacy to rare noncanonical splice (NCS) variants. The data types and the conclusions on their contributions to SEE are reviewed below and summarized in Table 2.

L73: Trials 102 and 103 were adequate and well-controlled phase 3 trials that enrolled 971 subjects aged 12 years and older with CF. Among the enrolled population, 95.7% of subject had a *CFTR* genotype that included at least one copy of the *F508del* variant, the most common variant in the U.S. CF population. Both Trials 102 and 103 demonstrated the noninferiority of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA to ELX/TEZ/IVA with respect to the ppFEV1 through 24 weeks. The noninferiority trial design and margin selected were reasonable.
L74: In addition, Trials 102 and 103 enrolled 42 subjects with 23 non-*F508del* triple combination responsive (i.e., responsive to ELX/TEZ/IVA based on in vitro data) variants. The subgroup analysis of these subjects was limited by sample size but was consistent with the overall population and supportive of the noninferiority of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA to ELX/TEZ/IVA in this population. Approval for this group of variants is also supported by in vitro data from prior development programs.
L75: To support an additional 259 *CFTR* variants, the Applicant submitted in vitro data utilizing the Fischer Rat Thyroid (FRT) cell system, a type of Ussing Chamber electrophysiology assay the Division has experience leveraging for regulatory actions for CF therapies. These data supported the inclusion of 154 *CFTR* variants that were previously demonstrated to be responsive to ELX/TEZ/IVA, TEZ/IVA, and/or IVA. These data also supported 31 new *CFTR* variants not responsive to currently approved therapies, as well as 74 *CFTR* variants that were recently added to the ELX/TEZ/IVA indication as part of a supplemental NDA uploaded to DARRTS on December 11, 2024 (DARRTS ID: 5493844).
L76: Additional in vitro data utilizing a human bronchial epithelial (HBE) cell system, another Ussing Chamber assay, supported the inclusion of a single variant, *N1303K*. HBE cells were used instead of FRT because the *N1303K* variant is not responsive to ELX/TEZ/IVA in the FRT system. A regulatory threshold to define effectiveness for this assay was established as part of the recently approved efficacy supplement for ELX/TEZ/IVA. The determination of effectiveness was made by comparing the response to VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA with the response to TEZ/IVA in *F508del/F508del* HBE cells.
L77: The efficacy and safety for 19 NCS variants were extrapolated from the overall population evaluated in Trials 102 and 103. These variants are expected to result in the production of small quantities of wild-type (normal function) CFTR, and extrapolation was supported, in part, by in vitro FRT data demonstrating that VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA augments wild-type CFTR function. In addition, there is clinical data demonstrating the benefit of ELX/TEZ/IVA in certain variants included in this group (Trial VX21-445-124, supplemental NDAs 212273/S012 and 217660/S003, uploaded to DARRTS on December 11, 2024 [DARRTS ID: 5493844]). Together,
L78: NDA 218730
L79: ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L80: these data support the conclusion that the expected response to treatment and benefits of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA would translate to patients with these rare variants.
L81: In addition, the efficacy and safety of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA were extrapolated from the adolescent and adult population to CF patients aged 6 through 11 years of age with a responsive *CFTR* variant. Extrapolation is supported by comparable drug systemic exposures between the 6- to 11-year-old population and the adolescent and adult population and the knowledge that CF is the same disease across age groups.
L82: ## 2. Benefit-Risk Assessment
L83: ## 2.1. Benefit-Risk Framework
L84: Table 3. Benefit-Risk Framework
L85: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Analysis of condition	CF is a rare, progressive, life-shortening autosomal recessive genetic condition that affects approximately 33,000 individuals in the United States. CF results from pathologic variants in the CFTR gene that lead to decreased or dysfunctional CFTR protein. CFTR aids in the regulation of salt and water absorption and secretion throughout the body. Lack of properly functioning CFTR leads to the clinical sequelae of CF disease: malabsorption of nutrients, inability to mobilize tenacious respiratory secretions, recurrent pulmonary infections, irreversible lung damage, and ultimately, premature respiratory failure. The majority of these pathologic changes start at birth. The median age of survival for a patient with CF is in the mid-40s.
The most common CFTR variant is F508del. In the United States, approximately 85% of CF patients carry at least one allele and 45% are homozygous for this variant.
CF is a rare, progressive, and life-shortening genetic disease. The pathologic changes associated with CF start early in life. The CFTR variants included in the proposed indication represent the majority of patients with CF in the United States.
L86: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Current treatment options	Although no cure exists, there are currently four therapies that affect CFTR processing or function that are approved for some of the CF population, including: ELX/TEZ/IVA (TRIKAFTA), TEZ/IVA (SYMDEKO), LUM/IVA (ORKAMBI), and IVA (KALYDECO).
The majority of CF patients in the United States are CFTR genotype eligible for at least one of the approved therapies; however, there remains a significant unmet need for alternative CF therapies, both for patients who are not currently eligible based on CFTR genotype as well as patients who are intolerant of the currently approved medications or with persistent symptom burden despite treatment.
The remainder of treatment options are aimed at managing the symptoms and sequelae of the underlying CF disease.
There remains an unmet need for therapies for CF patients who are not currently eligible for or are intolerant of an approved therapy that affects CFTR function or processing. There remains an additional need in patients with persistent symptom burden despite therapy with an approved medication.

Benefit	Trials 102 and 103 demonstrated the noninferiority of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA to ELX/TEZ/IVA with respect to pulmonary function as measured by the absolute change from baseline in the ppFEV1. Supportive secondary endpoints included change from baseline in SwCI, an important pharmacodynamic measure of CFTR function, and other endpoints that are clinically meaningful in a CF population (e.g., rate of pulmonary exacerbations, CFQ-R respiratory domain).
In vitro testing using the FRT system demonstrated an increase in chloride transport by ≥10% from baseline with treatment of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA or its components in certain CFTR variants.
Additional in vitro testing in CF HBE cells demonstrated the responsiveness of the tested variant by showing an increase in chloride transport comparable to that in other variants for which clinical efficacy data exists.
The clinical benefit of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA was demonstrated to be noninferior to ELX/TEZ/IVA for CF patients aged 12 years and older with a F508del variant or other responsive CFTR variant. VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA offers a once daily dosing regimen, compared to the twice daily dosing schedule of ELX/TEZ/IVA.
Efficacy in CF patients aged 6 through 11 years can be extrapolated from Trials 102 and 103 because of comparable systemic exposures between the 6- to 11-year-old population and the adolescent and adult populations and the knowledge that CF is the same disease across age groups. Results of exploratory efficacy endpoints from the open label trial in the 6- to 11-year-old population further support this conclusion.
The Applicant provided adequate data to support all proposed CFTR variants, which included clinical data, in vitro responsiveness data in FRT and HBE systems, and adequate support for the extrapolation of efficacy data in rare noncanonical splice variants not feasible to enroll in clinical trials because of rarity and not amenable to established in vitro systems.
L87: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L88: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Risk and risk management	The VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA AEs observed were generally consistent with the known safety profile of ELX/TEZ/IVA.
Specific safety analyses were performed, including analyses for liver function test elevation, rash, creatinine phosphokinase elevation, and neuropsychiatric events.
The safety data submitted suggest that VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA conveys a similar risk to ELX/TEZ/IVA, although the overall analysis is limited by the lack of placebo arm to assess the baseline rates of events.
A new signal for life-threatening drug-induced liver injury was recently characterized for ELX/TEZ/IVA, and the available data for VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA suggest the risk is likely similar. Limitations of the available data include lack of placebo arm and the ELX/TEZ/IVA run-in period for both Trials 102 and 103, which resulted in only ELX/TEZ/IVA-tolerant subjects eligible for enrollment.
The potential for DILI related to VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA was carefully evaluated in consultation with the Division of Hepatology and Nutrition. Rates of maximum transaminase elevations were greater for VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA compared to ELX/TEZ/IVA. We also identified five cases of nonjaundiced DILI events that were adjudicated as probably or possibly related to VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA.
The safety results from Trial 105 did not reveal any new safety signals or identify imbalances in AEs to suggest differential risks for children compared to adolescents or adults. However, given the single arm design and small sample size, interpretation of these results is limited.
The safety profile of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA appears similar to that of ELX/TEZ/IVA and includes the potential risk for DILI and liver failure.
A Boxed Warning for drug-induced liver injury and liver failure will be included in labeling, and increased postmarket surveillance will occur through an enhanced pharmacovigilance strategy. A REMS is not indicated at this time. There were no safety signals identified in the development program for VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA that outweigh the potential benefit for the treatment of CF.
Safety for the 6- through 11-year-old CF population was extrapolated from the adolescent and adult populations enrolled in the phase 3 development program based upon comparable systemic exposures.
L89: Abbreviations: AE, adverse event; CF, cystic fibrosis; CFTR, cystic fibrosis transmembrane conductance regulator; CFQ-R, cystic fibrosis questionnaire-revised; DILI, drug-induced liver injury; D-IVA, deutivacaftor; ELX, elexacaftor; FRT, Fischer Rat Thyroid; HBE, human bronchial epithelial; IVA, ivacaftor; LUM, lumacaftor; ppFEV1, percent predicted forced expiratory volume in 1 second; REMS, Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies; SwCl, sweat chloride; TEZ, tezacaftor; VNZ, vanzacaftor
L90: ## 2.2. Conclusions Regarding Benefit-Risk
L91: CF is a rare, progressive genetic condition that leads to premature respiratory failure caused by pathologic variants in the *CFTR* gene. The majority of patients in the United States are currently eligible for one of the four approved medications that affect CFTR processing or function Table 4. There remains an unmet need for new therapies for patients with CF, including those with ineligible *CFTR* variants and those who are intolerant of or incompletely treated by currently available therapies. VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA is a fixed-dose combination product with the same mechanism of action as ELX/TEZ/IVA. D-IVA increases the channel open probability of the CFTR channel, and VNZ and TEZ improve intracellular protein processing to allow for increased protein expression at the cell surface, thus resulting in an increase in overall apical anion transport.
L92: To support the NDA for VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA, the Applicant submitted the results from two phase 3, randomized, double-blind, ELX/TEZ/IVA-controlled, noninferiority trials. Both trials, VX20-121-102 (Trial 102) and VX20-121-103 (Trial 103) evaluated subjects with CF aged 12 years and older over 52 weeks and demonstrated the noninferiority of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA to ELX/TEZ/IVA with respect to pulmonary function as measured by the absolute change in ppFEV1 through 24 weeks. Additional secondary endpoints, including the rate of CF pulmonary exacerbations and the change in the cystic fibrosis questionnaire-revised respiratory domain, support the efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA. Interpretation of these endpoints is limited by lack of multiplicity control, but the results are generally supportive of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA and are discussed in more detail in Section 6.
L93: In addition, the Applicant submitted the results of secondary endpoints related to sweat chloride (SwCl) concentrations. SwCl is an important pharmacodynamic marker of CFTR function; however, endpoints based on SwCl are not clinically meaningful as they do not measure how a patient feels, functions, or survives and have not been validated as surrogate endpoints. As such, they cannot be used to demonstrate comparative efficacy. Both phase 3 trials demonstrated a statistically significant reduction in the change from baseline in SwCl with VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA treatment compared to ELX/TEZ/IVA; however, it is unclear how these changes translate to clinically meaningful outcomes. The Applicant also submitted results of responder analyses assessing the proportion of subjects with a week 24 SwCl below 60mmol/L and below 30mmol/L, which were also statistically significant in the prespecified analyses. The data submitted to support the clinical relevance of these responder analyses, however, was insufficient. Overall, the SwCl endpoints support that VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA has comparable on target pharmacodynamic effects to ELX/TEZ/IVA. For additional discussion of SwCl, refer to Section 6.3.2.
L94: There was an adequate safety database to support this review. However, a significant limitation of the safety database stemmed from the noninferiority design, which eliminated a placebo arm and necessitated enrolled subjects be tolerant of ELX/TEZ/IVA, including during a four-week run-in period. Since 86.3% of the population had been prescribed ELX/TEZ/IVA or a medication containing its components prior to enrollment, the generalizability of safety results to ELX/TEZ/IVA naïve or intolerant patients is limited. Overall, the available data support that the safety of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA is similar to that of ELX/TEZ/IVA. The potential risk of liver injury
L95: related to VNZ/TEX/D-IVA was a major focus of the safety review. Overall, there were higher rates of transaminase elevation and liver-related adverse events leading to drug discontinuation in subjects treated with VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA compared to ELX/TEZ IVA. There were no cases of Hy's Law or liver failure reported, but there were five cases of nonjaundiced drug-induced liver injury adjudicated as probably or possibly related to VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA exposure. A multidisciplinary team recommended that the potential risk for drug-induced liver injury and liver failure with VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA be communicated to prescribers in a Boxed Warning with further monitoring through an enhanced pharmacovigilance strategy. Refer to Section 7.7.2 for details.
L96: The efficacy and safety of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA were extrapolated to the 6- to 11-year-old population from the adolescent and adult population studied in Trials 102 and 103. The Applicant submitted an interim report of the final clinical data from the 6- to 11-year-old cohort from Trial VX21-121-105 (Trial 105), an open-label, multicohort, 2-part trial in pediatric CF subjects aged 1 through 11 years. Extrapolation of efficacy and safety in this population is justified because systemic exposures between the 6- to 11-year-old population and the adolescent and adult populations are comparable and because CF is the same disease across age groups. Exploratory efficacy endpoints from Trial 105 were also supportive of the efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA in this population, and a review of safety data from Trial 105 did not reveal any new concerning safety signals in this population.
L97: The data submitted to support the satisfaction of 21 Code of Federal Regulations 300.50, the 'combination rule' was considered adequate and included the clinical demonstration of noninferiority of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA to ELX/TEZ/IVA, as well as the available historical clinical trial data with IVA, TEZ/IVA, and ELX/TEZ/IVA. Further support comes from in vitro data demonstrating a greater effect of the VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA triple combination versus the mono-components and dual-components alone. Refer to Section 6.3.1 for further discussion.
L98: To support the *CFTR* variant based indication, the Applicant submitted adequate data for the individual variants. This included in vitro electrophysiology models utilizing FRT and CF HBE cells, as well as adequate support to justify the potential for benefit in 19 rare NCS variants that are not amenable to enrollment in clinical trials because of relative rarity and cannot be evaluated in existing in vitro models. Refer to Section 6.3.3 for further discussion.
L99: The overall risk-benefit assessment supports the approval of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA for the treatment of CF in patients aged 6 years and old with a copy of *F508del* or other responsive variants in the *CFTR* gene.
L100: ## II. Interdisciplinary Assessment
L101: ## 3. Introduction
L102: Cystic fibrosis (CF) is an autosomal recessive, progressive disease affecting over 33,000 people in the United States and 90,000 people worldwide (CFF 2023). CF is caused by pathologic variants in the cystic fibrosis transmembrane conductance regulator (*CFTR*) gene that leads to decreased or dysfunctional CFTR protein, an anion channel in the apical epithelial membrane. In the lungs, the CF-mediated alterations in anion and fluid transport result in the accumulation of thick, tenacious mucus leading to a chronic cycle of inflammation, obstruction, and infection that ultimately progresses to premature respiratory failure, the leading cause of death in this population (CFF 2023). In addition, there are significant extrapulmonary manifestations characteristic of CF, including malabsorption, chronic sinus disease, poor bone health, liver dysfunction, and a form of diabetes (CFF 2023).
L103: There are over 700 variants in the *CFTR* gene that are associated with CF disease causation, although over 2,000 variants have been identified (CFF; JHU; and HSC 2024). The most common CF-causing variant in the United States is *F508del* (F), a variant that results in improper protein folding leading to increased degradation (Cheng et al. 1990). In CF patients with genotype information available in the United States, approximately 85% of individuals are heterozygous for *F508del* and 44% of individuals are *F508del* homozygous (CFF 2023). The reported prevalence of the next most commonly reported *CFTR* variants in the United States is significantly lower with no other variant reported in &gt;5.0% of the population in 2023 (CFF 2023).
L104: Currently, there is no cure for CF and, historically, CF-directed therapies were focused on the relief of symptoms and sequelae of the disease. More recently, medications targeting the underlying pathophysiologic defect in CFTR function and general intracellular protein processing of CFTR have been developed and approved for CF subpopulations based on *CFTR* variants as reviewed below in Table 4.
L105: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L106: **Table 4. Medications Approved for the Treatment of CF That Affect CFTR Processing or Function**
L107: Active Ingredient	Trade Name	FDA-Approved Indication
Ivacaftor (IVA)	KALYDECO tablets and granules	Treatment of CF in patients aged 1 month and older who have at least one mutation in CFTR gene that is responsive to IVA based on clinical and/or in vitro dataa
Lumicaftor/Ivacaftor (LUM/IVA)	ORKAMBI tablets and granules	Treatment of CF in patients aged 1 year and older who are homozygous for the F508del mutation in the CFTR gene
Tezacaftor/Ivacaftor (TEZ/IVA)	SYMDEKO tablets	Treatment of CF in patients aged 6 years and older who are homozygous for F508del mutation or who have at least one mutation in CFTR gene that is responsive to TEZ/IVA based on in vitro data and/or clinical evidenceb
Elezacaftor/Tezacaftor/Ivacaftor (ELX/TEZ/IVA)	TRIKAFTA tablets and granules	Treatment of CF in patients aged 2 years and older who have at least one F508del mutation in the CFTR gene or another mutation in the CFTR gene that is responsive based on in vitro data
L108: Source: Clinical Review from Drugs@FDA, accessed on Oct 3, 2024
L109: a Does not include *F508del*
L110: b Does not include patients heterozygous for *F508del* without another responsive *CFTR* mutation
L111: Abbreviations: CF, cystic fibrosis; CFTR, cystic fibrosis transmembrane conductance regulator; ELX, elezacaftor; IVA, ivacaftor; LUM, lumicaftor; TEZ, tezacaftor
L112: Ivacaftor (IVA, Trade name: KALYDECO), the first medication targeting CFTR function, was approved in 2012 but limited at that time to patients aged 6 years and older with a *G551D CFTR* variant, the third most prevalent *CFTR* variant reported in the United States (Vertex Pharmaceuticals 2012). Initial expansion of the IVA indication to additional variants was based on clinical data; however, given the rarity of remaining variants, subsequent indication expansions were based on in vitro studies leveraging Ussing chamber electrophysiology experiments with Fischer Rat Thyroid (FRT) cells expressing normal and mutant CFTR protein (Durmowicz et al. 2018). Subsequently, all other medications that directly affect CFTR processing or function have had the indications expanded to include additional variants using FRT data that demonstrate an increase in chloride transport by \ge 10\% of normal from baseline. At the time of this review, approximately 92% of U.S. CF patients are eligible for a medication that affects CFTR processing or function based on their genotype and age (CFF 2023).
L113: Elezacaftor/tezacaftor/ivacaftor (ELX/TEZ/IVA), approved in 2019 (Vertex Pharmaceuticals 2019), accounted for 93% of prescriptions for medications that affect CFTR processing or function reported in 2023 and is standard-of-care therapy for those with eligible *CFTR* variants (CFF 2023). It is the most effective therapy for CF that targets the underlying defect in the disease.
L114: These medications have provided significant clinical benefits to many patients with CF with responsive *CFTR* variants (Volkova et al. 2020). However, despite these advances, there remains a significant ongoing disease burden in CF and the CF population would benefit from safe and effective alternative therapies.
L115: Vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor (VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA), an oral fixed-dose combination (FDC) tablet, has the same mechanism of action as ELX/TEZ/IVA. As part of its clinical development program, ELX/TEZ/IVA had demonstrated a large, sustained clinical effect on the forced
L116: expiratory volume in 1 second (FEV_1), a clinically meaningful endpoint for which there is regulatory precedent in CF (Heijerman et al. 2019; Middleton et al. 2019). Given the severity of the underlying disease, the effect size of ELX/TEZ/IVA, and its market prevalence within the CF population, the Applicant and Division agreed upon a clinical development program that included two pivotal trials designed to assess the noninferiority of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA compared to ELX/TEZ/IVA. The trial design and assessment of the noninferiority margin are reviewed in Section 6.2.2.1 with an in-depth review of the clinical protocols included in Section 15.1.

L125: ## 3.2. Approach to the Clinical Review
L126: The Applicant submitted data from two phase 3 clinical trials, Trials 102 and 103, as the primary support for the safety and efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA in CF. These trials were both international, multicenter, randomized, double-blind, ELX/TEZ/IVA-controlled trials evaluating the efficacy and safety of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA over 52 weeks. The design of the phase 3 clinical development program is reasonable. The in-depth review of the clinical protocols is included in Section 15.1.
L127: In addition, the Applicant submitted an interim analysis from Trial 105 to support the use of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA in pediatric CF patients ages 6 through 11. Trial 105 is an ongoing, two-part, open-label, multicohort, pediatric, pharmacokinetic (PK) and safety study of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA in CF subjects 1 through 11 years of age. The submitted interim analysis included the final clinical data for the 6- to 11-year-old cohorts (Cohorts A1 and B1). An in-depth review of the
L128: clinical protocol for Trial 105 is included in Section 15.2. As with other similar medications in this population, the efficacy and safety in the 6- to 11-year-old CF population is primarily extrapolated from adolescents and adults. The efficacy-related endpoints and safety profile from the 6- to 11-year-old cohorts were also reviewed in comparison to the adolescent and adult data to assess for consistency or the presence of new signals.
L129: FDA biostatisticians performed efficacy analyses to confirm the data shown in support of the Applicant's primary and secondary efficacy endpoints. The review of the efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA is in Section 6. The clinical reviewer and FDA clinical data scientist performed the safety review, found in Section 7. The clinical pharmacology team, including reviewers from the Division of Applied Regulatory Science, reviewed dose ranging trials and in vitro analyses used to support efficacy as well as data supporting the contribution of each component to the fixed-dose combination, found in Section 6.3.1.
L130: The approach to establishing substantial evidence of effectiveness (SEE) is reviewed in Section 3.3, and a summary of the clinical trials submitted by the Applicant in support of the efficacy and safety of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA is reviewed below in Table 5.

L160: ## 5. Pharmacologic Activity, Pharmacokinetics, and Clinical Pharmacology
L161: ## 5.1. Nonclinical Assessment of Potential Effectiveness
L162: VNZ and TEZ bind to different sites on the CFTR protein and have an additive effect in facilitating the cellular processing and trafficking of select mutant forms of CFTR (including *F508del*-CFTR) to increase the amount of CFTR protein delivered to the cell surface compared to either molecule alone. Similar to IVA, deuterated ivacaftor (D-IVA) potentiates the channel open probability (or gating) of the CFTR protein at the cell surface. The combined effect of VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA is increased quantity and function of CFTR at the cell surface, resulting
L163: NDA 218730
L164: ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L165: in increased CFTR activity as measured by CFTR-mediated chloride transport in CF human bronchial epithelial (HBE) cell cultures derived from donor CF lungs.
L166: In vivo pharmacodynamic studies were not performed. The totality of in vitro pharmacology data submitted was considered sufficient to characterize the pharmacological activity of VNZ, TEZ, and IVA and inform labeling for the indication of CF.
L167: ## 5.2. Clinical Pharmacology/Pharmacokinetics
L168: **Table 7. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetics**
L169: Characteristic	Drug Information
Pharmacologic Activity
Established pharmacologic class (EPC)	VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA is a combination of deutivacaftor, a CFTR potentiator, tezacaftor, and vanzacaftor, indicated for the treatment of cystic fibrosis (CF) in patients aged 6 years and older who have at least one F508del variant in the CFTR gene or a variant in the CFTR gene.
Mechanism of action	VNZ and TEZ bind to different sites on the CFTR protein and have an additive effect in facilitating the cellular processing and trafficking of select mutant forms of CFTR (including F508del-CFTR) to increase the amount of CFTR protein delivered to the cell surface compared to either molecule alone.
D-IVA potentiates the channel open probability (or gating) of the CFTR protein at the cell surface.
Active moieties	The active moieties are VNZ, TEZ, M1-TEZ (similar potency compared to TEZ), D-IVA, and M1-D-IVA (20% of potency compared to D-IVA).
QT prolongation	Following a single dose of 60 mg VNZ (Form A, which resulted in six times the C_{max} value when compared to the C_{max} following 20 mg VNZ dose in Form D in FDC tablet), no clinically relevant effects on prolongation of the QTc interval were observed in the thorough QT study, Study VX22-121-013.
Refer to the approved label of ELX/TEZ/IVA (Trade name: TRIKAFTA). At doses up to three times the maximum recommended dose of TEZ and IVA, the QT/QTc interval in healthy subjects was not prolonged to any clinically relevant extent. In vitro results showed that IVA inhibits about 22% of hERG current at 8 \muM. Given the hydrogen to deuterium replacements from IVA to D-IVA, which is unlikely to change its 3-dimensional conformation, the high plasma protein binding of IVA and D-IVA (>99%), and the in vivo C_{max,ss} value around 3 – 6 \muM for IVA and D-IVA, it is reasonable to extrapolate the in vivo QT results from IVA to D-IVA following the therapeutic dose of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA.
L170: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L171: Characteristic	Drug Information
Bioanalysis	
General Information
Methods for the quantitation of VNZ in human K2-EDTA plasma using LC-MS/MS Detection were developed and validated. A method for the quantitation of VNZ in human urine using LC/MS/MS was developed and validated. The bioanalytical assays were considered acceptable.
Validation reports for D-IVA and TEZ were previously reviewed and found adequate in the original application for NDA 210491 TEZ/IVA and NDA 212273 ELX/TEZ/IVA.

Healthy subjects versus patients	
The popPK estimated effect does not necessarily suggest a PK difference between healthy subjects and CF Patients. This observation is likely attributable to the relatively small number of healthy subjects in the final data and confounding due to healthy subjects receiving only VNZ Form A.

Drug exposure at steady state following the therapeutic dosing regimen (or single dose, if more relevant for the drug)	
Table 8. Drug Exposure Values
General Information	Vanzacaftor	Tezacaftor	Deutivacaftor
Mean (SD) Cmax,ss, mcg/mL	0.812 (0.344)	6.77 (1.24)	2.33 (0.637)

Source: Clinical pharmacology reviewer
Abbreviations: AUC0-24h,ss, area under the concentration-time curve from time 0 to 24 hours at steady-state; Cmax, maximum plasma concentration at steady-state; SD, standard deviation

Range of effective dose(s) or exposure	
In a phase 2 dose-ranging study for VNZ (Study VX18-121-101), VNZ [Form A] 10 mg showed the numerically greatest improvement of ppFEV1 response compared to placebo group when compared to 5 mg and 20 mg.
In a phase 2 dose-ranging study for D-IVA (Study VX18-561-101), D-IVA 250 mg and D-IVA 150 mg QD resulted in similar absolute change from baseline in ppFEV1 compared to IVA 150 mg Q12h treatment, active control. In addition, treatment with D-IVA 250 mg QD yielded numerically greater improvements in SwCI response compared to D-IVA 150 mg QD.
In the pivotal phase 3 trials (Trials VX20-121-102 and VX20-121-103), VNZ [Form D] 20 mg/TEZ 100 mg/D-IVA 250 mg FDC QD was evaluated and demonstrated noninferiority to ELX/TEZ/IVA, the current standard of care, on the primary efficacy endpoint of absolute change from baseline in ppFEV1 through Week 24.
Pediatric PK and safety trial (Trial VX21-121-105) demonstrated that the exposures (trough concentrations) of each APIs and their active metabolites in subjects aged 6 through 11 years following the proposed doses (VNZ 20 mg/TEZ 100 mg/D-IVA 250 mg for body weight ≥40 kg and VNZ 12 mg/TEZ 60 mg/D-IVA 150 mg for body weight <40 kg) were generally within the range of adults and adolescents following VNZ 20 mg/TEZ 100 mg/D-IVA 250 mg.

Maximally tolerated dose or exposure	
VNZ [Form A] up to 60 mg QD for 10 days was investigated in healthy subjects in Study VX17-121-001.
Up to 300 mg once daily dose of TEZ for 7 days has been investigated in QT Study VX15-661-010.
A single dose of D-IVA 300 mg was the maximally tolerated dose in healthy subjects in Study CP656.1001.
L172: Characteristic	Drug Information
Dose proportionality	
Study VX17-121-001 Part A showed that using suspension formulation, VNZ exposure increases dose proportionally from 5 to 60 mg following single dose administration (i.e., AUC_{\text{inf}} increases 14.6-fold when dose increases 12-fold) in healthy subjects. Part B from the same study showed VNZ exposure increases slightly greater than dose proportionally following administration of multiple oral doses of VNZ QD for 10 days, which VNZ AUC_{\text{tau}} increased 8.5-fold from 10 mg QD to 60 mg QD.
Refer to the clinical pharmacology review of TEZ/IVA (dated Feb 2, 2018), exposures of tezacaftor (administered alone or in combination with ivacaftor) increase in an approximately dose proportional manner with increasing doses from 10 mg to 300 mg once daily.
Study VX18-561-001 showed that D-IVA exposure increases dose proportionally with ascending dose from 50 to 250 mg following 5 days once daily treatment (AUC_{\text{tau}} increases 6-fold).

Accumulation	
Study VX17-121-001 showed accumulation ratio of AUC_{\text{tau}} ranged 3.5-5.5 for VNZ in healthy subjects.
Study VX18-561-001 showed accumulation ratio of AUC_{\text{tau}} ranged 1.2-1.7 for D-IVA in healthy subjects.
Refer to the approved label of TRIKAFTA, the accumulation ratio of TEZ is 2.1 at steady state following 100 mg once daily dosing regimen.

Time to achieve steady-state	
Study VX17-121-001 Part C showed that following once daily regimen in the context of TC, the VNZ C_{\text{trough}} value was stabilized by Day 10 in healthy subjects. Trial VX20-121-102 showed that VNZ C_{\text{trough}} value was stabilized since Day 15 (i.e., the earliest PK sampling time from this trial) following the to-be-marketed FDC tablet once daily administration in subjects with CF. Given the effective elimination half-life of 93 hours for VNZ, it is reasonable to conclude that the steady state of VNZ is reached on Day 10.
Study VX18-561-001 showed that following once daily regimen, D-IVA C_{\text{trough}} value was stabilized around Days 4-5 in healthy subjects, Trial VX20-121-102 and 103 showed that D-IVA C_{\text{trough}} value was stabilized since Day 15 (i.e., the earliest PK sampling time from these trials) following the to-be-marketed FDC tablet once daily administration in subjects with CF. Given the effective elimination half-life of 19 hours for D-IVA, it is reasonable to conclude that the steady state of D-IVA is reached no later than Day 5.
Study VX17-121-001 Part C showed that following once daily regimen in the context of TC, TEZ C_{\text{trough}} value was stabilized on Day 7. Trials VX20-121-102 and VX20-121-103 showed that TEZ C_{\text{trough}} value was stabilized since Day 15 (i.e., the earliest PK sampling time from these trials) following the to-be-marketed FDC tablet once daily administration in subjects with CF. Given the elimination half-life of 22 hours for TEZ, it is reasonable to conclude that the steady state of TEZ is reached no later than Day 7.

Bridge between to-be-marketed and clinical trial/study formulations	
To-be-marketed formulation VNZ [Form D]/TEZ/D-IVA FDC tablet (dosing strength of 10 mg/50 mg/125 mg and 3 mg/20 mg/50 mg) was used in pivotal phase 3 trials and pediatric Trial 105.
During the development, VNZ [Form A] was used in phase 1 and phase 2 studies. Studies VX19-121-003 and VX21-121-005 demonstrated that the VNZ C_{\text{max}} and AUC from Form D (to-be marketed) was approximately 50% of that from Form A. The results from Studies VX19-121-003 and VX21-121-005 supported the dose selection of 20 mg VNZ [Form D] in phase 3 trials.
L173: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L174: Characteristic	Drug Information
Absorption
Bioavailability	Absolute bioavailability of VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA were not determined.
Tmax	The median (min, max) of Tmax of VNZ and D-IVA were 10 (4, 12) hours (Study VX17-121-001, Part B) and 4 (3, 6) hours (Study VX18-561-001), respectively.
Food effect (fed/fasted)	In Study VX21-121-005, food effect (high- and low-fat) on VNZ 20 mg/TEZ 100 mg/D-IVA 250 mg FDC tablet was evaluated. High-fat food increases AUC and Cmax of VNZ by 5.8- and 6.3-fold, TEZ by 1.2- and 1.1-fold, D-IVA by 4.2- and 5.4-fold, respectively. Low-fat food increases AUC and Cmax of VNZ by 3.7- and 3.9-fold, TEZ by 1.1- and 1.2-fold, D-IVA by 3.3- and 3.4-fold, respectively. Food did not relevantly change Tmax of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA.
Distribution
Volume of distribution	After a single oral dose of 2 VNZ 10-mg/TEZ 50-mg/D-IVA 125-mg FDC tablets, the mean (SD) apparent volumes of distribution were 90.4 (31.3) L for VNZ, 123 (43.2) L for TEZ, and 157 (47.3) L for D-IVA.
Plasma protein binding	Plasma protein binding of VNZ and D-IVA are greater than 99%. Plasma protein binding of TEZ is approximately 99%.
Drug as substrate of transporters	Based on in vitro data, VNZ is not a substrate of P-gp, BCRP, OATP1B1, or OATP1B3.
Based on in vitro data, D-IVA and M1-D-IVA are substrates of P-gp and BCRP but are not substrates of OATP1B1 or OATP1B3.
Refer to the approved label of ELX/TEZ/IVA, TEZ is a substrate for P-gp and BCRP, but not a substrate of OATP1B1 or OATP1B3.
Elimination
Mass balance results	Unchanged VNZ accounted for most of the total circulating radioactivity in plasma and there were no major circulating metabolites of VNZ. VNZ was mainly cleared by metabolism in humans. The total excretion (in urine and feces) of the radiolabeled dose over 408 hours (17 days) was approximately 92.1%, with fecal excretion being the major route of elimination accounting for 91.6% and urinary excretion accounting for 0.5% (Study VX20-121-004).
Refer to the mass balance Study VX13-661-005, the recovery rate of 14C-labeled TEZ is 85.9% at 21 days post-single dose with 72.2% and 13.7% excreted in feces and urine, respectively. No unchanged TEZ is detected in urine and unchanged TEZ only constitute 30.1% of the dose in feces.
A human mass balance trial of D-IVA was not performed. However, it is expected that the excretion pathway for D-IVA is similar to IVA. Refer to the approved label of IVA (trade name: KALYDECO), following oral administration, the majority of ivacaftor (87.8%) is eliminated in the feces after metabolic conversion. There was minimal urinary excretion of ivacaftor as unchanged parent.
L175: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L176: Characteristic	Drug Information
Clearance	Mean (SD) apparent clearance were 1.18 (0.455) L/hr for VNZ, 0.937 (0.338) L/hr for TEZ, and 6.52 (2.77) L/hr for D-IVA
Half-life	Mean (% CV) of the elimination half-life of VNZ and D-IVA were 50.4 (32.3) hours (Study VX17-121-001, Part B) and 21.8 (28.1) hours (Study VX18-561-001), respectively. The effective half-life of VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA are 92.8 (30.2), 22.5 (5.9), and 19.2 (8.7) hours, respectively.
Metabolic pathway(s)	VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA are primarily metabolized by CYP3A4/5.
Body weight and age	
Intrinsic Factors and Specific Populations
Pediatric PK and safety trial (Study VX21-121-105) demonstrated that the exposures (trough concentrations) of each APIs and their active metabolites in subjects aged 6 through 11 years following the proposed doses (VNZ 20 mg/TEZ 100 mg/D-IVA 250 mg for body weight \ge40 kg and VNZ 12 mg/TEZ 60 mg/D-IVA 150 mg for body weight <40 kg) were generally within the range of adults and adolescents following VNZ 20 mg/TEZ 100 mg/D-IVA 250 mg.
Weight was identified as the main factor contributing to the differences in the PK between adult and pediatric subjects. Age was not identified as a significant covariate after the body weight adjustment

Renal impairment	No dedicated renal impairment study has been conducted for VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA FDC tablet. It is reasonable that no dose adjustment for subjects with mild or moderate renal impairment due to the minimal renal excretion for all three active ingredients. In addition, popPK showed similar exposure of VNZ/TEZ/F-IVA between healthy subjects and subjects with mild/moderate renal impairment. Therefore, the proposal for use with caution in people with CF with severe RI or end-stage renal disease is also reasonable.
Hepatic impairment	
Following administration of a single dose of VNZ 10mg/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg in hepatic impairment (HI) Study VX21-121-008, the total exposure (AUC_{inf}) of VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA decreased 30%, remained comparable, and decreased 20%, respectively, in subjects with moderate hepatic impairment when compared to matched subjects with normal hepatic function. Similarly, the free drug exposure (AUC_{u,inf}) of VNZ and D-IVA decreased 20% and 40%, respectively, in subjects with moderate HI when compared to matched subjects with normal hepatic function
Based on these results and hepatotoxicity of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA, it is reasonable that no dosage adjustment in patients with mild hepatic impairment is proposed. It is reasonable that the Applicant proposed VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA use in patients with moderate HI should only be considered when there is a clear medical need, and the benefit exceeds the risk. If used, no dose adjustment is recommended. VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA use is not recommended in patients with severe hepatic impairment.
L177: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L178: Characteristic	Drug Information
Moderate or strong CYP3A4 inhibitor	
Clinical DDI studies evaluated the effects of the strong CYP3A inhibitor itraconazole on the PK of VNZ, TEZ and D-IVA. Coadministration with itraconazole increased VNZ AUC by 3.5- to 10.5-fold, TEZ AUC by 4.0- to 4.5-fold and D-IVA AUC by 11-fold.
PBPK analysis was used to predict the interaction effect of moderate CYP3A inhibitors. Simulations indicated that coadministration with moderate CYP3A inhibitors may increase the AUC of VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA by approximately 2.4- to 4-fold, 2-fold, and 3- to 5-fold, respectively.
Simulations indicated that predicted fold changes in AUC with moderate and strong CYP3A inhibitors were similar between children aged 6 through 11 years and adults at the proposed therapeutic doses of VNZ and D-IVA, assuming the exposure levels of CYP34 inhibitors are similar between adults and pediatrics.
The dose of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA should be reduced when coadministered with strong and moderate CYP3A inhibitors, as follows:

L179: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L180: Characteristic	Drug Information
Drug Interaction Liability (Drug as Perpetrator)
Inhibition/induction of metabolism	
D-IVA may inhibit CYP2C9 (in vitro IC_{50} value of 15.6 \muM with R value of 1.03). The risk for clinical inhibition was not ruled out using PBPK analysis. Therefore, monitoring of the international normalized ratio (INR) during coadministration of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA with warfarin is recommended. Other medicinal products for which exposure may be increased by VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA include glimepiride and glipizide; these medicinal products should be used with caution.
Based on in vitro and in vivo results, VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA have low potential to inhibit other CYP enzymes. VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA have low induction potential for CYP enzymes at clinically relevant exposures.

Inhibition/induction of transporter systems	
In vitro studies indicate VNZ is not a substrate of P-gp, BCRP, OATP1B1, and OATP1B3.
In vitro studies indicate D-IVA is a substrate of P-gp (efflux ratio of 6.7 at 1 \muM, which is similar to value 5.6 of IVA) and BCRP (net efflux ratio of 42.1 at 50 \muM, which is similar to value of 32.5 of IVA), but not OATP1B1 and OATP1B3.
In vitro studies indicate TEZ is a substrate of P-gp (efflux ratio of 4.18), BCRP (efflux ratio of 4.62), and OATP1B1, but not OATP1B3.
In vitro results showed that VNZ has potential to inhibit BCRP transporter (I_{\text{gut}}/IC_{50}=532) following the recommended dose (i.e., 20 mg). Therefore, a postmarketing requirement DDI study to evaluate the effect of VNZ on PK of a sensitive BCRP substrate is recommended.
In vitro results showed that TEZ has low potential to inhibit P-gp, BCRP, OCT2, OAT1, or OAT3.
In vitro results showed similar P-gp and BCRP inhibition potencies between IVA and D-IVA. Study VX14-661-006 showed that coadministration of ivacaftor or tezacaftor/ivacaftor with digoxin, a sensitive P-gp substrate, increased digoxin AUC by 1.3-fold, consistent with weak inhibition of P-gp by ivacaftor. Therefore, the review team agree that a similar P-gp DDI-related language can applied from IVA to D-IVA (i.e., when used concomitantly with digoxin or other substrates of P gp with a narrow therapeutic index such as cyclosporine, everolimus, sirolimus, and tacrolimus, caution and appropriate monitoring should be used).

Immunogenicity (if Applicable)
Bioanalysis	NA
Incidence	NA
Clinical impact	NA
L181: Source: Clinical pharmacology reviewer
L182: Abbreviations: API, active pharmaceutical ingredient(s); AUC, area under the concentration-time curve; AUC_{\text{inf}}, area under the concentration-time estimated to infinity; AUC_{\tau_{\text{au}}}, area under the concentration-time curve during the dosing interval; BCRP, breast cancer resistance protein; CF, cystic fibrosis; CFTR, cystic fibrosis transmembrane conductance regulator; C_{\text{max}}, maximum plasma concentration; C_{\text{max,ss}}, maximum plasma concentration at steady-state; C_{\text{trough}}, lowest plasma concentration at steady-state; CV, coefficient of variation; CYP, cytochrome P450; DDI, drug-drug interaction; D-IVA, deutivacaftor; ELX, elexacaftor; FDC, fixed-dose combination; hERG, human ether-à-go-go-related gene; HI, hepatic impairment; IC_{50}, half maximal inhibitory concentration; IVA, ivacaftor; LC-MS/MS, liquid chromatography with tandem mass spectrometry; M, metabolite; NA, not applicable; OAT, organic anion transporter; OCT, organic cation transporter; P-gp, P-glycoprotein; PBPK, population-based pharmacokinetics; PK, pharmacokinetic; popPK, population pharmacokinetics; ppFEV1, percent predicted forced expiratory volume in 1 second; Q12h, once every 12 hours; QD, once daily; QT, QT interval; QTc, corrected QT interval; RI, renal impairment; SD, standard deviation; SwCl, sweat chloride; TEZ, tezacaftor; T_{\text{max}}, time to maximum concentration; VNZ, vanzacaftor
L183: ## 6. Efficacy (Evaluation of Benefit)
L184: ## 6.1. Assessment of Dose and Potential Effectiveness
L185: The proposed dose of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA FDC product is VNZ [Form D] 20 mg/TEZ 100 mg/D-IVA 250 mg once daily (QD) for subjects 12 years of age or older and subjects 6 through 11 years with body weight \ge40 kg; and VNZ [Form D] 12 mg/TEZ 60 mg/D-IVA 150 mg QD for subjects 6 through 11 years with body weight &lt;40 kg.
L186: The dose selection of VNZ and D-IVA for phase 3 trials were primarily determined based on the two phase 2 dose-ranging studies (Study VX18-121-101 and Study VX18-561-101 for VNZ and D-IVA, respectively), and two phase 1 relative bioavailability studies (Study VX19-121-003 and Study VX21-121-005). The dose selection of TEZ is based on the approved TEZ dose of ELX/TEZ/IVA (Trade name: TRIKAFTA, NDA 212273 and NDA 217660) for the treatment of CF.
L187: The proposed pediatric dose for subjects 6 through 11 years of age was primarily supported by Trial VX22-121-105 and population PK modeling and simulations.
L188: ## 6.1.1. Dose Selection of Vanzacaftor
L189: Dose-ranging results from part 1 of the phase 2 study, Study VX18-121-101, demonstrated a numerically higher percent predicted forced expiratory volume in 1 second (ppFEV1) improvement from baseline on Day 29 following VNZ [Form A] 10 mg QD when compared to other VNZ doses in the context of triple combination (TC) with TEZ 100 mg QD and IVA 150 mg every 12 hours (q12h) in CF subjects with *F508del* /minimal function (MF) variants. In addition, a near maximal response on sweat chloride (SwCl) reduction was observed between 10 mg [Form A] and 20 mg [Form A]. Therefore, a dose of 10 mg VNZ [Form A] QD was selected for further evaluation in phase 3 studies. However, (b) (4)
L190: Additional PK bridging studies (Study VX19-121-003 and Study VX21-121-005) were conducted between the two forms, and the results showed that the bioavailability of VNZ [Form D] was only about 50% of that of VNZ [Form A] following the same dose administration. Based on these results, a dose of 20 mg VNZ [Form D] QD was eventually selected and carried into phase 3 trials.
L191: ## VNZ Dose-Ranging Study VX18-121-101 (Study 101)
L192: Study 101 provided primary support for VNZ dose selection for the pivotal phase 3 studies. Study 101 was a 2-part, randomized, double-blind, placebo- (Part 1) or TEZ/IVA-controlled (Part 2), proof-of-concept trial of VNZ in CF subjects. VNZ [Form A] was used in both Part 1 and Part 2 of this trial.
L193: The primary endpoint was the absolute change in ppFEV1 through Day 29. Key secondary endpoints were the absolute change from baseline in SwCl and the absolute change from baseline in cystic fibrosis questionnaire-revised (CFQ-R) respiratory domain (RD) score at Day 29.
L194: In Part 1, CF subjects with *F508del*/MF variants were enrolled. The treatment groups were VNZ [Form A] 5, 10, and 20 mg QD in the context of TC with TEZ 100 mg QD and IVA 150 mg q12h in comparison to triple-placebo for 4 weeks. Subjects with no previous treatment with medications that affect CFTR processing or function within 28 days before the first dose were randomized in a 2:2:1:1 ratio to the TC-VNZ 20 mg, TC-VNZ 10 mg, TC-VNZ 5 mg, and placebo arms. In total, 58 subjects were randomized and received at least 1 dose of trial drug. In Part 2, CF subjects with *F508del*/*F508del* variant with no previous treatment with a medication that affects CFTR processing or function within 28 days before the start of TEZ/IVA run-in period (4 weeks) were enrolled. VNZ [Form A] 20 mg QD/TEZ 100 mg QD/IVA 150 mg q12h was evaluated in comparison to TEZ 100 mg QD/IVA 150 mg q12h. Subjects were randomized in a 2:1 ratio to the TC-VNZ 20 mg and TEZ/IVA arms. Twenty-nine (29) subjects were enrolled and 28 completed the treatment.
L195: VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA treatment for 4 weeks in CF subjects with *F508del*/MF or *F508del*/*F508del* genotypes resulted in improvements in ppFEV1, SwCl, and CFQ-R RD score (secondary efficacy endpoints) from the respective baseline when compared to the control groups (placebo for Part 1 and TEZ/IVA for Part 2) as presented in Table 10. In Part 1, among three doses of VNZ [Form A], the 10 mg dose showed the numerically greatest improvement in ppFEV1 response compared to placebo group, versus VNZ 5 mg and 20 mg.

L201: The clinical pharmacology review team acknowledges that Study VX21-121-006 demonstrated VNZ maximum plasma concentration (C_{max}) and area under the concentration-time curve (AUC) increased 48% and 54%, respectively, when VNZ was administered in TC with TEZ/D-IVA compared to VNZ administered alone. There is a concern whether this increase of VNZ systemic exposure up to 50% may change the efficacy/safety profile of VNZ in the TC context. However, since the dose ranging trial of VNZ (Study 101) was conducted in the TC context, the dose
L202: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L203: ranging results directly support the 10 mg VNZ [Form A] dose in the TC context that was used in the phase 3 trials.
L204: Therefore, the Applicant's decision to carry 10 mg VNZ [Form A] (later translated into 20 mg VNZ [Form D], see below) into phase 3 trials is reasonable.
L205: ## **VNZ Formulation Bridging Study VX19-121-003 (Study 003)**
L206: VNZ dose selection was also based on an assessment of the relative bioavailability of VNZ [Form A] physical formulation compared to VNZ [Form D] physical formulation in Study 003. Study 003 was a phase 1, open-label, randomized, 2-sequence, 2-period crossover study to evaluate the relative bioavailability of a single dose FDC tablet of VNZ [Form D] 20 mg/TEZ 100 mg/D-IVA 150 mg (test) compared to single dose VNZ [Form A] 20 mg, TEZ 100 mg, and D-IVA 150 mg coadministered as separate tablets (reference) under fed conditions.
L207: Twenty healthy subjects were randomized and received at least one dose of study drug. The PK metrics and relative bioavailability of VNZ (Form A versus Form D), TEZ, and D-IVA after single-dose administration as an FDC tablet or as separate tablets was statistically evaluated. The geometric least squares mean ratios and their associated 90% CIs are provided for exposure of each active pharmaceutical ingredient (API) in Table 11. The exposure (AUC and C_{max}) of VNZ following Form A was approximately 50% of the exposure following FDC tablet, which contained VNZ Form D. Based on these results, a dose of VNZ [Form D] 20 mg QD provides similar exposure as the VNZ [Form A] 10 mg QD dose, which was evaluated in VNZ dose-ranging trial, Study 101.

L215: ## **VNZ Formulation Bridging Study VX21-121-005 (Study 005)**
L216: Relative bioavailability (BA) of VNZ [Form A] physical formulation compared to VNZ [Form D] physical formulation was further evaluated at steady state in Part A of Study 005. Study 005 Part A was a phase 1, open-label, randomized, 2-sequence, 2-period, crossover trial to evaluate the relative bioavailability of FDC tablet of VNZ [Form D] 20 mg/TEZ 100 mg /D-IVA 250 mg (test) compared to single dose VNZ [Form A] 10 mg, TEZ 100 mg, and D-IVA 250 mg coadministered as separate tablets (reference) at steady-state. Trial drug was given under fed conditions.
L217: Eighteen healthy subjects were randomized and received at least one dose of trial drug. The PK metrics and relative BA of VNZ (Form A versus Form D), TEZ, and D-IVA after single-dose administration as an FDC tablet or as separate tablets was statistically evaluated. The geometric least squares mean ratios and their associated 90% CIs are provided for exposure of each API in **Table 12**. Exposures of VNZ 20 mg [Form D], TEZ 100 mg, and D-IVA 250 mg were similar when trial drug was given as FDC tablets relative to when it was given as separate tablets as VNZ 10 mg [Form A], TEZ 100 mg, and D-IVA 250 mg.

L225: Based on the relative BA results from Study 003 and 005, a single dose of VNZ [Form D] 20 mg achieves similar exposure compared to a single dose of 10 mg VNZ [Form A] in the context of TC. Therefore, the decision to carry 20 mg VNZ [Form D] into phase 3 trials is considered reasonable by the clinical pharmacology review team.
L226: Exposure-response analysis (Report t299) showed VNZ doses of 5, 10, and 20 mg daily administered with TEZ/D-IVA were associated with no additional increase in ppFEV1 beyond the increases achieved with ELX/TEZ/IVA treatment during the run-in period. The VNZ 20 mg dose administered in combination with TEZ/D-IVA was associated with a reduction of up to 7.11 mmol/L in SwCl beyond the reduction achieved by ELX/TEZ/IVA. The clinical meaning of
L227: this small numerical difference is unclear. In addition, the analysis showed a numerically greater reduction of SwCl from baseline by VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA and ELX/TEZ/IVA in subjects with *F508del/F508del* and *F508del/MF* variants than other genotypes (e.g., *F508del/gating [G]* or *F508del/residual function [RF]*), probably related to higher SwCl baseline levels in subjects with *F508del/F508del* and *F508del/MF* variants. Refer to Section 6.2.3.2. for additional definitions of these variant types.
L228: ## 6.1.2. Dose Selection of Deutivacaftor
L229: The dose of D-IVA 250 mg QD was selected for the phase 3 trials in subjects \ge12 years of age because this dose resulted in numerically greater improvements in SwCl response with similar ppFEV1 response when compared to D-IVA 150 mg QD in the dose-ranging study, Study VX18-561-101.
L230: ## Study VX18-561-101 (Study 561-101)
L231: Study 561-101 was a phase 2, randomized, double-blind, parallel-group, and active-controlled study of D-IVA monotherapy in CF subjects who have a gating variant and were previously taking a stable dose of IVA. Four dose levels of D-IVA (25, 50, 150, and 250 mg) were evaluated to provide a broad range of exposure for exposure-response analyses.
L232: The primary endpoint was the absolute change in ppFEV1 at Week 12. Key secondary endpoint was the absolute change from baseline in SwCl at Week 12.
L233: In total, 77 subjects who were on stable IVA treatment were randomized and 75 received at least one dose of study drug. Two lower dose treatment groups (25 mg and 50 mg) were prematurely discontinued because of high drop-off rate, which was presumably related to insufficient CFTR modulation. Results from Study 561-101 demonstrated treatment with either D-IVA 250 mg QD or D-IVA 150 mg QD resulted in similar absolute values of ppFEV1 change from baseline compared to IVA 150 mg q12h treatment (Table 13). However, treatment with D-IVA 250 mg QD resulted in numerically greater improvements in SwCl response compared to D-IVA 150 mg QD. Therefore, the Applicant's choice of D-IVA 250 mg QD for further evaluation in phase 3 trials was reasonable.

L238: The clinical pharmacology review team acknowledges that Study VX21-121-006 demonstrated D-IVA Cmax and AUC increased 18% and 27%, respectively, when D-IVA was administered in TC with VNZ/TEZ compared to D-IVA administered alone. There is a concern whether this
L239: increase of D-IVA systemic exposure of up to 27% may change the efficacy/safety profile of D-IVA in the TC context. Based on the flat dose-response relationship over D-IVA 150 mg to D-IVA 250 mg on ppFEV1 response observed from Study 561-101, it is unlikely that observed increase of systemic exposure would contribute additional efficacy of D-IVA in the context of TC.
L240: ## 6.1.3. Dose Selection for Pediatric Subjects 6 Through 11 Years Old
L241: ## Trial VX21-121-105 (Trial 105)
L242: Trial 105 was an open-label, 2-part (Parts A and B), multicohort (Cohorts 1, 2, and 3), multicenter trial evaluating the PK, safety, tolerability, and efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA therapy in CF subjects 1 through 11 years of age who carry at least one triple combination responsive (TCR) *CFTR* variant. Cohorts A1 and B1 evaluated subjects 6 through 11 years of age.
L243: The primary objective of Trial 105 was to evaluate the PK, safety, and tolerability of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA and relevant metabolites when dosed in TC in pediatric CF subjects.
L244: Subjects in Cohort A1 received VNZ 10 mg QD/TEZ 50 mg QD/D-IVA 125 mg QD for 22 days. Subjects who participated in Cohort A1 were permitted to participate in Cohort B1 if they met eligibility criteria (refer to Section 15 for complete enrollment criteria). A review of safety, tolerability, and available PK data was completed by an internal team after Cohort A1 completed treatment to determine the doses and weight cutoffs for Cohort B1.
L245: In Cohort A1, 17 subjects 6 through 11 years of age were randomized and received at least one dose of trial drug. All subjects received VNZ 10 mg/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg QD, which is half the dose for CF subjects \ge12 years of age. The comparison results showed that the mean plasma concentration observed at the end of dosing interval (C_{\text{trough}}) values in Cohort A1 (on Day 22) of three active ingredients and their active metabolites in subjects 6 through 11 years of age were generally lower than that from adults and adolescents in the pivotal phase 3 Trials 102 and 103 as presented in Table 14.
L246: Therefore, the pediatric VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA doses were increased in both body weight tiers in Cohort B1 with the same adult/adolescent dose for subjects \ge40 kg and VNZ 12 mg/TEZ 60 mg/D-IVA 150 mg for subjects &lt;40 kg. Following the new dosing algorithm, overall trough concentrations were within the range for each of the analytes previously observed in CF subjects \ge12 years of age in pivotal phase 3 Trials 102 and 103.

L259: ## 6.2. Clinical Studies/Trials Intended To Demonstrate Efficacy
L260: ## 6.2.1. Results of Pooled Analyses
L261: The evaluation of efficacy was based on the analyses of the individual trials. Pooled efficacy analyses will not be discussed in this review.
L262: ## 6.2.2. Trial VX20-121-102 (Trial 102)
L263: ## 6.2.2.1. Design, Trial 102
L264: Trial VX20-121-102 (Trial 102) was a phase 3, multicenter, randomized, double-blind, parallel-group, ELX/TEZ/IVA-controlled, noninferiority trial evaluating the safety and efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA in subjects with CF 12 years of age and older. The trial included a 4-week run-in period during which all subjects received ELX/TEZ/IVA to establish a reliable on-treatment (ELX/TEZ/IVA) baseline for the treatment period. Subjects were then randomized 1:1 to receive VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA or ELX/TEZ/IVA for a 52-week treatment period. Randomization was stratified by: age at screening, ppFEV1 during the run-in period (Day -14 clinic assessment), SwCl during the run-in period (Day -14 clinic assessment), and prior use of ELX/TEZ/IVA or medications that contain one or more of its components. The full review of the trial protocol design, subject population, objectives, and endpoints is in Section 15.1.
L265: The dosages evaluated in the phase 3 trials were:
L266: - Active control: ELX 200 mg QD/TEZ 100 mg QD/IVA 150 mg q12h
- Treatment: VNZ 20 mg QD/TEZ 100 mg QD/D-IVA 250 mg QD
L267: The primary objective of Trial 102 was to evaluate the efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA in adolescent and adult CF subjects. The efficacy endpoints are listed below:
L268: ## Primary Endpoint
L269: - Absolute change from baseline in ppFEV1 through Week 24
L270: ## Key Secondary Endpoints
L271: - Absolute change from baseline in sweat chloride (SwCl) through Week 24
- Proportion of subjects with SwCl &lt;60mmol/L through Week 24 (pooled with data from Trials 102 and 103)
- Proportion of subjects with SwCl &lt;30mmol/L through Week 24 (pooled with data from Trials 102 and 103)
L272: ## Other Secondary Endpoints
L273: - Number of CF pulmonary exacerbations (PEx) through Week 52
- Absolute change from baseline in CFQ-R RD score through Week 24
- Absolute change from baseline in ppFEV1 through Week 52
- Absolute change from baseline in SwCl through Week 52
- Proportion of subjects with SwCl &lt;60mmol/L through Week 24
- Proportion of subjects with SwCl &lt;30mmol/L through Week 24
- Safety and tolerability assessments based on adverse events (AEs), clinical laboratory values, electrocardiograms, vital signs, and pulse oximetry
L274: ELX/TEZ/IVA and other medications that affect CFTR processing or function have contributed to significant clinical benefits in the CF population and are currently used by over 80% of patients in the United States (CFR 2023). The Applicant designed a noninferiority (NI) trial with ELX/TEZ/IVA as the active control. Given the magnitude of treatment effect and pervasive use of ELX/TEZ/IVA as the standard of care, the Applicant argued use of placebo control would have been unethical and infeasible, with which the Division agreed.
L275: There was not a previously accepted NI margin: i.e., an amount of loss of pulmonary function, as measured by forced expiratory volume in one second (FEV1) (or any other lung function measure), by which a new drug could be worse than a reference drug with established efficacy to determine noninferiority in CF. The Applicant proposed an NI margin for absolute change in ppFEV1 of -3.0% supported by data from the ELX/TEZ/IVA development program, which included the following rationale:
L276: - For the population eligible for Trial 102 (*F508del/MF*), the lower bound of the 95% CI of the treatment effect for ELX/TEZ/IVA was approximately 12% for the ppFEV1. According to the FDA guidance for industry *Non-Inferiority Clinical Trials to Establish Effectiveness* (November 2016), preservation of at least 50% of the conservative effect of 12% (M1) is recommended as a clinically acceptable NI margin (M2), i.e., \le 6\% (equal to 50% of 12% effect size). Thus, the Applicant proposed 3.0% as the NI margin, which the Division agreed is reasonable. Refer to Section 15 for additional details on the NI margin.
L277: All other efficacy endpoints were analyzed using superiority comparisons, some of which were controlled for multiplicity (refer to Section 15.1 for details). As reviewed in Section 6.3.2, the key secondary endpoints were limited to SwCl-based endpoints and were the only secondary endpoints analyzed under multiplicity control. The Division views SwCl as an important pharmacodynamic marker of CFTR function but advised the Applicant to consider additional non-SwCl key secondary endpoint(s) that represent clinically meaningful outcomes (e.g., PEx, CFQ-R). The Applicant included assessments of these clinically meaningful endpoints, but they were maintained as ‘other’ secondary endpoints and not included in the statistical analysis hierarchy. The results of these other endpoints are reviewed in more detail in Section 6.2.2.4 and Section 16.2.
L278: Overall, the trial design, endpoint selection, and endpoint classifications were reasonable to demonstrate noninferiority in a CF population with respect to ppFEV1.
L279: ## 6.2.2.2. Eligibility Criteria, Trial 102
L280: The eligibility criteria for Trial 102 were appropriate for capturing a population of adults and adolescents with stable CF. The *CFTR* variant-based eligibility for Trial 102 included subjects heterozygous for the *F508del* and minimal function (MF) *CFTR* variants, defined as either a variant that results in no translated CFTR protein or a variant that is nonresponsive to TEZ, IVA, or TEZ/IVA based on in vitro testing.
L281: The key inclusion criteria included:
L282: - Ages 12 years and older
- Heterozygous *F508del* and a MF *CFTR* variant
- Percent predicted FEV1 (Quanjer et al. 2012):Subjects receiving ELX/TEZ/IVA at screening: \ge 40\% and \le 90\%
- Subjects not receiving ELX/TEZ/IVA at screening: \ge 40\% and \le 80\%
L283: The key exclusion criteria included:
L284: - History of intolerance to trial drug including ELX/TEZ/IVA
- History of comorbidity including but not limited to hepatic cirrhosis, moderate to severe hepatic impairment, solid organ or hematological transplantation
- Acute upper or lower respiratory tract infection, infectious PEx of CF, or changes in therapy (including antibiotics) for sinopulmonary disease
L285: The trial population was consistent with similar clinical development programs in CF, including Trial VX17-445-102 used to support the registration of ELX/TEZ/IVA. These similarities in the population further support the use of a noninferiority trial design with ELX/TEZ/IVA as the active control. The exclusion of subjects unable to tolerate ELX/TEZ/IVA is reasonable and appropriate given the design of the trial; however, this is a significant limitation that affects the generalizability of safety results. Additional information regarding the trial's population is included in Section 15.
L286: ## 6.2.2.3. Statistical Analysis Plan, Trial 102
L287: The primary efficacy analysis compared VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA to ELX/TEZ/IVA using a mixed-effects model for repeated measures (MMRM) adjusted for treatment, visit, age at screening (&lt;18 versus \ge 18 years of age), and treatment-by-visit interaction, with baseline ppFEV1 and baseline SwCI as continuous covariates. The primary result obtained from the model was the estimated treatment difference through Week 24, which was estimated by averaging Weeks 16 and 24. The adjusted mean difference with a 2-sided 95% CI and the 1-sided P value for NI were provided. The primary null hypothesis was to be rejected, and noninferiority demonstrated, if the
L288: lower bound of the 95% CI was \ge 3.0\%. Intercurrent events of premature treatment discontinuation and use of non-trial medications that affect CFTR processing or function for &gt;3 days were handled with a treatment policy strategy. Conditional on the observed data and covariates, missing data was assumed to be missing at random, and its impact was assessed by tipping point analyses. The analysis was conducted on the full analysis set (FAS) population, defined as all randomized subjects who carry the intended *CFTR* genotype and received at least one dose of trial drug.
L289: To address multiplicity in the analysis of the efficacy endpoints, the Applicant pre-specified a hierarchical order for the primary endpoint and key secondary endpoints of absolute change from baseline in SwCl through Week 24, and proportion of subjects with SwCl &lt;60mmol/L and SwCl &lt;30mmol/L through Week 24 on the pooled with data from Trials 102 and 103. See Section 15.1 for additional information.
L290: ## 6.2.2.4. Results of Analyses, Trial 102
L291: ## Subject Enrollment and Disposition
L292: A total of 435 subjects were enrolled in Trial 102. During the run-in period, 37 (8.5%) of subjects discontinued the trial and did not enter the treatment period. The most common reason reported for discontinuation (22, 5.1%) during the run-in period was an adverse event of which the majority were related to CF pulmonary exacerbations. Following the run-in period, 405 subjects were randomized and 398 (98.3%) received at least 1 dose of the trial drug and are considered the FAS. A summary of the subject enrollment and disposition is included in Table 15.

L297: The majority of subjects completed treatment (367, 92.2%), and the overall trial (375, 94.2%). The rates of treatment discontinuation were balanced between groups, although there were small numeric differences in the primary reasons for treatment discontinuation (not shown in Table 15): e.g., adverse events were reported in 10 (5.0%) subjects in the ELX/TEZ/IVA group compared to 4 (2.0%) subjects in the VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA group; subject refusal of further dosing was reported in 2 (1.0%) subjects in the ELX/TEZ/IVA group compared to 5 (2.6%) subjects in the VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA group. Any imbalance between groups is unlikely to affect the analysis given the overall low rate of treatment discontinuation.
L298: The rates of trial discontinuation were also balanced between groups, and the main reasons cited included withdrawal of consent (5 [2.5%] subjects in ELX/TEZ/IVA group and 5 [2.6%] subjects in VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA group) and adverse events (4 [2.0%] subjects in ELX/TEZ/IVA group and 4 [2.0%] subjects in VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA group). The rates of treatment and trial discontinuation were low. Rates of trial completion and treatment discontinuation in Trial 102 were similar to other phase 3 programs in this population.
L299: ## **Subject Demographics**
L300: The population enrolled in Trial 102 had a mean age of 30.8 years (range 12.2 to 71.6 years), was 59.0% male, and 14.3% adolescents (12 to &lt;18 years). The mean age of Trial 102 was older than phase 3 trials from the ELX/TEZ/IVA program, but not unreasonable based on the known demographics of the CF population. L319: Overall, the baseline CF disease characteristics were balanced between the treatment groups and reasonable for a stable adolescent and adult CF population. The overall use of other CF background symptomatic therapies was lower than the phase 3 trials for ELX/TEZ/IVA, with the exception of inhaled hypertonic saline and inhaled bronchodilators. This difference is not unexpected and is likely reflective of changes in prescribing practices over time in the context of ELX/TEZ/IVA availability. The rate of positive *Pseudomonas aeruginosa* culture within the last 2 years was also lower than the phase 3 trials of ELX/TEZ/IVA and is consistent with recently published data about the overall CF population (CFF 2023). These differences in baseline disease characteristics are not significant enough to affect the appropriateness of the reference trials for the noninferiority comparison.
L320: Trial 102 limited enrollment to subjects who were heterozygous for *F508del* and an MF variant. The past medical history and concomitant medications were reviewed and are consistent with a CF adult and adolescent population and balanced between treatment groups. Additional information regarding subject genotype, past medical history, and concomitant medications is included in Section 16.1.1.
L321: ## Analysis of Efficacy – Primary Endpoint
L322: Trial 102 was designed as a noninferiority trial with a designated margin of -3.0% for the primary endpoint of ppFEV1. The least squares (LS) mean difference for change from baseline in ppFEV1 through Week 24 was 0.2% (95% CI: -0.7%, 1.1%) in VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA compared to ELX/TEZ/IVA based on the FAS population, as shown in Table 18. Since the lower bound of the 95% CI was greater than -3.0%, VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA was demonstrated to be noninferior to ELX/TEZ/IVA in ppFEV1.

L329: The Applicant specified that the primary endpoint would be analyzed through Week 24, defined as the average of the values from Weeks 16 and 24. During the development of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA, the Division recommended the use of a landmark analysis at Week 24 to better capture durability of treatment effect. The Applicant maintained the through 24-week analysis; therefore, the statistical reviewer and analyst performed an additional landmark analysis using an MMRM analysis for change from baseline in ppFEV1 at Week 24. The landmark analysis at Week 24 resulted in an LS mean difference (95% CI) of 0.3% (-1.3%, 0.8%), which supported the noninferiority of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA to ELX/TEZ/IVA, as well. In addition, the robustness of the analysis results regarding missing data was supported by the tipping point sensitivity analysis. See Section 16.2 for details.
L330: Although the landmark analysis was post hoc and exploratory, the results were supportive of the treatment effect of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA. For future development programs, the Division continues to recommend landmark analyses for ppFEV1 endpoints.
