L2152: **Table 127. Model Evaluation of TEZ Population PK Analysis**
L2153: Final Model	Summary	Acceptability (FDA's Comments)
Software and version	NONMEM®, Version 7.5.1	Acceptable
Estimation algorithm	First-order conditional estimation with \eta-\epsilon interaction	Acceptable
Model structure	2-compartment model with first-order absorption and an absorption lag time	Acceptable
Model parameter estimates	Table 7, Report T271	Acceptable
Uncertainty and variability (RSE, IIV, shrinkage, bootstrap)	Except for Vp, IIV on Vp, and the additive residual variances, all RSE's (fixed and random effects) were <30%; shrinkage for IIV on CL was <10%.	Acceptable
BLQ for parameter accuracy	BLQ observations (1.29%) were excluded. No impact of BLQ on PK objectives given the low percentage of BLQ observations	Acceptable
GOF, VPC	Section 6.3, Report T271:Diagnostic plots (Figures S5 to S36) indicated that the final model adequately described all the data with no discernible systematic bias, including that for the 6 through 11 years age group.
Section 6.4, Report T271:VPC (Figures S49 to S52) and pcVPC (Figures 10 to 13) results showed simulated TEZ concentrations were similar to observed concentrations at the 5th, 50th, and 95th percentiles across most of the concentration-time profile, both overall or when stratified by relevant covariates such as age, weight, and genotype.
Acceptable
Significant covariates and clinical relevance	Body weight was the only covariate that had a clinically meaningful impact on TEZ disposition.
L2154: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L2155: Final Model	Summary	Acceptability (FDA's Comments)
Analysis based on simulation (optional)	Simulations based on the final population PK model for TEZ supported the justification of candidate weight-based dosing regimens of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA TC in CF patients \ge6 years of age with a 40 kg weight cutoff (Table 8; and Figures 14, 15, S66, S71, Report T271).
Additional weight cutoffs were also evaluated:35 kg: Table S3; and Figures S60 and S72, Report T271
30 kg: Table S6; and Figures S63 and S73, Report T271.

Simulations based on the final population PK models showed exposures of TEZ and M1-TEZ in adult CF subjects with MHI and no TEZ dose adjustment were in the same range as subjects without MHI (Figure S74, Report T271). This result supports that, based on PK, no TEZ dose adjustment was needed for adult CF subjects with MHI.
Simulations based on the final population PK models for TEZ and M1-TEZ supported the justification of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA dose adjustment regimens with reduced daily dose and/or dosing frequency for children 6 through 11 years with CF in the presence of coadministered CYP3A4 inhibitors (Figure S76 and S88, Report T271).
Acceptable

L2218: General Information	Details
Objectives of PPK analysis	Characterize the PK disposition of M1-TEZ in subjects 6 years of age and older using population methodology;
Quantify the effects of covariates, including body weight and CFTR genotype, as sources of PK variability in TEZ and M1-TEZ;
Summarize AUCss, 0–24 for M1-TEZ from the studied dosing regimen for phase 3 subjects due to sparse PK sampling;
Evaluate if a 40 kg weight cutoff for all studied ages resulted in similar exposures to adults;Simulate M1-TEZ exposures for a VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA dosing regimen with a 40 kg weight cutoff in children 6 through 11 years with CF;
Simulate M1-TEZ exposures for a VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA dosing regimen with a 40 kg weight cutoff in adolescents 12 through 17 years with CF;

Simulate exposures of M1-TEZ to inform dosing and/or weight bounds for weight cutoffs other than 40 kg for subjects ≥6 years of age;
Simulate exposures of M1-TEZ for different dosing regimens to inform dosing for adults with CF with moderate hepatic impairment (MHI); and
Simulate exposures of M1-TEZ for different dosing regimens to inform dosing for children 6 through 11 years coadministered a strong or moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor.

L2223: **Table 133. Model Evaluation of M1-TEZ Population PK Analysis**
L2224: Final Model	Summary	Acceptability (FDA's Comments)
Software and Version	NONMEM®, Version 7.5.1	Acceptable
Estimation Algorithm	First-order conditional estimation with \eta-\epsilon interaction (FOCEI)	Acceptable
Model Structure	2-compartment model (sequentially developed using the empirical Bayes estimates from the TEZ model)	Acceptable
Model Parameter Estimates	Table 16, Report T271	Acceptable
Uncertainty and Variability (RSE, IIV, Shrinkage, Bootstrap)	Except for the residual variances for phase 1/2 studies, all RSE's (fixed and random effects) were <30%; shrinkage for IIV on CL was <10%.	Acceptable
BLQ for Parameter Accuracy	BLQ observations (1.31%) were excluded. No impact of BLQ on PK objectives given the low percentage of BLQ observations	Acceptable
GOF, VPC	Overall, diagnostic plots (Figures S106 to S137) indicated that the final model adequately described all the data with no marked systematic bias, including that for the 6 through 11 years age group.
Section 7.4, Report T271:VPC (Figures S150 to S153) and pcVPC (Figures 19 to 22) results showed simulated M1-TEZ concentrations were similar to observed concentrations at the 5th, 50th, and 95th percentiles across most of the concentration-time profile both overall or when stratified by relevant covariates such as age, weight, and genotype.
Acceptable
Significant Covariates and Clinical Relevance	Body weight was the only covariate that had a clinically meaningful impact on M1-TEZ disposition	Acceptable
L2225: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L2226: Final Model	Summary	Acceptability (FDA's Comments)
Analysis Based on Simulation (optional)	Simulations based on the final population PK model for TEZ supported the justification of candidate weight-based dosing regimens of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA TC in CF patients \ge6 years of age with a 40 kg weight cutoff (Table 17; and Figures 23, 24, S167, and S172, Report T271).
Additional weight cutoffs were also evaluated:35 kg: Table S15; and Figures S161 and S173, Report T271
30 kg: Table S18; and Figures S164 and S174, Report T271.

Simulations based on the final population PK models showed exposures of TEZ and M1-TEZ in adult CF subjects with MHI and no TEZ dose adjustment were in the same range as subjects without MHI (Figure S175, Report T271). This result supports that, based on PK, no TEZ dose adjustment was needed for adult CF subjects with MHI.
Simulations based on the final population PK models for TEZ and M1-TEZ supported the justification of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA dose adjustment regimens with reduced daily dose and/or dosing frequency for children 6 through 11 years with CF in the presence of coadministered CYP3A4 inhibitors (Figure S177 and S189, Report T271).
Acceptable

Uncertainty and Variability (RSE, IIV, Shrinkage, Bootstrap)	Except for the TCR genotype and additive residual variances, all RSE's (fixed and random effects) were <30%; shrinkage for IIV on CL was <10%.	
BLQ for Parameter Accuracy	BLQ observations (3.9%) were excluded. No impact of BLQ on PK objectives given the low percentage of BLQ observations	Acceptable
GOF, VPC	
Section 7.1.6, Report T406:
Diagnostics plots (Figures 11.33 to 11.52) indicated that the final model adequately described all the data with no discernible systematic bias, including that for the 6 through 11 years age group.
Section 7.1.7, Report T406:
pcVPCs indicated that the final model captured the observed data reasonably well overall (Figure 7.6). The pcVPCs stratified by age group (Figure 7.7), weight group (Figure 7.8), and genotype (Figure 7.9) showed that the model adequately captured both the central tendency and the variability of the steady-state data.
There are missing data points in Figure 11.36. The Applicant provided clarification and/or correction to 11.36 in IR dated Aug 25, 2024. The update figures are acceptable.
Significant Covariates and Clinical Relevance	Body weight was the only covariate that had a clinically meaningful impact on D-IVA disposition.	Acceptable
L2292: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L2293: Final Model	Summary	Acceptability (FDA's Comments)
Analysis Based on Simulation (optional)	Simulations based on the final population PK model for D-IVA supported the justification of candidate weight-based dosing regimens of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA TC in CF patients \ge6 years of age with a 40 kg weight cutoff (Table 11.7; and Figures 11.136, 11.139, 11.148, 11.165, Report T406).
Additional weight cutoffs were also evaluated:35 kg: Table 11.11; and Figures 11.142 and 11.168, Report T406
30 kg: Table 11.12; and Figures 11.145 and 11.171, Report T406.

Simulations based on the final population PK models showed exposures of D-IVA in adult CF subjects with MHI and no TEZ dose adjustment were in the same range as subjects without MHI (Figure 11.174, Report T406). This result supports that, based on PK, no D-IVA dose adjustment was needed for adult CF subjects with MHI.
Simulations based on the final population PK models for D- supported the justification of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA dose adjustment regimens with reduced daily dose and/or dosing frequency for children 6 through 11 years with CF in the presence of coadministered CYP3A4 inhibitors (Figures 11.177, 11.181, 11.185 and 11.189, Report T406).
Acceptable

L2349: ## Population Pharmacokinetic Assessment Summary
L2350: **Table 143. Data Structure of M1-D-IVA and M6-D-IVA Population PK Analysis, Report T406**
L2351: General Information	Details
Objectives of PPK analysis	To characterize the PK disposition of M1-D-IVA and M6-D-IVA (D-IVA metabolites) in subjects 6 years and older using population methodology;
To quantify the sources of variability;
To summarize predicted AUC0-24h from the studied dosing regimen for phase 3 subjects due to sparse PK sampling;
To evaluate if a 40 kg weight cutoff for all studied ages resulted in similar exposures to adults;To simulate D-IVA and its metabolites exposures for a VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA TC dosing regimen with a 40 kg weight cutoff in children 6 through 11 years with CF;
To simulate D-IVA and its metabolites exposures for a VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA TC dosing regimen with a 40 kg weight cutoff in adolescents 12 through 17 years with CF;

To simulate exposures of D-IVA and its metabolites to inform dosing and/or weight bounds for weight cutoffs other than 40 kg for subjects ≥6 years of age;
To simulate exposures of D-IVA for different dosing regimens to inform dosing for CF subjects with MHI;
To simulate exposures of D-IVA for different dosing regimens to inform dosing for children 6 through 11 years with CF coadministered a strong or moderate cytochrome P4503A4 (CYP3A4) inhibitor

Study included	VX18-121-101, VX20-121-102, VX20-121-103, VX21-121-105, VX17-561-001, VX18-561-101, VX16-770-018
D-IVA Dose(s) included	25 mg, 50 mg, 100 mg, 125 mg, 150 mg, 250 mg QD (Table 5.4, Report T406)
Population included	Healthy, CF (F/MF, F/F, F/G, F/RF, TCR, and F/other genotypes)

Estimation Algorithm	First-order conditional estimation with \eta-\epsilon interaction (FOCE-I)	Acceptable
Model Structure	Linear, one-compartment M1-D-IVA, and 2-compartment M6-D-IVA popPK model with sequential conversion of D-IVA to M1-D-IVA, and M1-D-IVA to M6-D-IVA	Acceptable
Model Parameter Estimates	Table 7.5, Report T406	Acceptable
Uncertainty and Variability (RSE, IIV, Shrinkage, Bootstrap)	Except for the weight exponent on V/F of M1 and IIV on V/F of M1, and additive residual variances, all RSE's (fixed and random effects) were <30%; shrinkage for IIV on CL was <10%.	
BLQ for Parameter Accuracy	BLQ observations (4.1% for M1 and 6.7% for M6) were excluded.
No impact of BLQ on PK objectives	Acceptable
L2358: Final Model	Summary	Acceptability (FDA's Comments)
GOF, VPC	
Section 7.2.6, Report T406:
Diagnostics plots indicated that the final model provided an adequate description of the D-IVA metabolite concentration data, as judged by visual inspection of the observed versus the population and the individual predictions, respectively (Figures 11.103 to 11.133).
Section 7.2.7, Report T406:
pcVPCs indicated that the final model captured the observed data reasonably well overall (Figure 7.20). The pcVPCs stratified by age group (Figure 7.21), weight group (Figure 7.22), and genotype (Figure 7.23) showed that the model adequately captured both the central tendency and the variability of the steady-state data.
There are missing data points in Figure 11.109. The Applicant provided clarification and/or correction to 11.109 in IR dated Aug 30, 2024.
There are missing data points in Figure 11.110. The Applicant provided clarification and/or correction to 11.110 in IR dated Aug 25, 2024.
The updated figures are acceptable.

Significant Covariates and Clinical Relevance	
Figure 7.17, Report T406 (Forest plot)
Body weight was the only covariate that had a clinically significant impact on D-IVA disposition requiring dose adjustment to align exposures in CF subjects <40 kg.
Acceptable

## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L2360: Final Model	Summary	Acceptability (FDA's Comments)
Analysis Based on Simulation (optional)	Simulations based on the final population PK model for D-IVA supported the justification of candidate weight-based dosing regimens of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA TC in CF patients ≥6 years of age with a 40 kg weight cutoff (Table 11.7; and Figures 11.136, 11.139, 11.148, 11.165, Report T406).
Additional weight cutoffs were also evaluated:35 kg: Table 11.11; and Figures 11.142 and 11.168, Report T406
30 kg: Table 11.12; and Figures 11.145 and 11.171, Report T406.

Simulations based on the final population PK models showed exposures of D-IVA in adult CF subjects with MHI and no TEZ dose adjustment were in the same range as subjects without MHI (Figure 11.174, Report T406). This result supports that, based on PK, no D-IVA dose adjustment was needed for adult CF subjects with MHI.
Simulations based on the final population PK models for D- supported the justification of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA dose adjustment regimens with reduced daily dose and/or dosing frequency for children 6 through 11 years with CF in the presence of coadministered CYP3A4 inhibitors (Figures 11.177, 11.181, 11.185 and 11.189, Report T406).
Acceptable

L2534: ## 14.5.2. Physiologically Based Pharmacokinetic Modeling Review
L2535: ## Executive Summary
L2536: The objective of this review is to evaluate the adequacy of the Applicant's PBPK modeling analyses to predict the following:
L2537: - The DDI effect of moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors (fluconazole, erythromycin, and verapamil) on the PK of single and multiple doses of VNZ and D-IVA in adults.
- The DDI effect of strong and moderate CYP3A4 inducers on the PK of multiple doses of VNZ and D-IVA in adults.
- The DDI effect of strong and moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors on the PK of multiple doses of VNZ and D-IVA in children aged 6 through 11 years.
- To determine dose adjustment regimens when using the to-be marketed FDC tablet (VNZ Form D 10 mg /TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) in the presence of moderate or strong CYP3A inhibitors in adults.
- The DDI effect of VNZ and D-IVA on CYP2C9 substrates.
L2538: The Divisions of Inflammation and Immune Pharmacology and Pharmacometrics have reviewed the PBPK submission (Reports T183, T322, T333, response to Information Requests, August 23, 2024, and September 6, 2024, SDN 0019 and 0022, Module 1.11.3) to conclude the following:
L2539: - Simulations predicted 2.4- to 3.9-fold increase in the AUC of VNZ when single or multiple doses of VNZ was coadministered with multiple doses of a moderate CYP3A inhibitor.
- Simulations predicted 2.9- to 4.8-fold increase in area under the concentration-time estimated to infinity (AUC_{inf}) and 3.1- to 5.1-fold increase in AUC_{0-24,ss} of D-IVA when a single dose or multiple doses of D-IVA was coadministered, respectively, with a moderate CYP3A inhibitor.
- Simulations predicted 82% and 90% reduction in VNZ and D-IVA exposure, respectively, when coadministered with rifampin, a strong CYP3A inducer. Efavirenz, a moderate CYP3A inducer, was predicted to decrease the exposure to VNZ and D-IVA by 69% and 73%, respectively.
- Simulation predicted increase in VNZ C_{max,ss} and AUC_{0-24,ss} by approximately 10- and 11-fold in children 6 through 11 years weighing \ge 40 kg, and 10- and 9.5-fold in children 6 through 11 years weighing &lt; 40 kg, respectively, when multiple doses of VNZ were coadministered with itraconazole, a strong CYP3A inhibitor. Similarly, D-IVA C_{max,ss} and AUC_{0-24,ss} is predicted to increase by 7.4- and 14-fold in children 6 through 11 years weighing \ge 40 kg, and 6.8- and 14-fold in children 6 through 11 years weighing &lt; 40 kg, respectively, when D-IVA was coadministered with itraconazole.
- Simulation predicted increase in VNZ C_{max,ss} and AUC_{0-24,ss} by 2.5- and 2.6-fold in children 6 through 11 years weighing \ge 40 kg, and 2.5- and 2.6-fold in children 6 through 11 years
L2540: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L2541: weighing &lt;40 kg when multiple doses of VNZ were coadministered with fluconazole, a moderate CYP3A inhibitor. The increase in D-IVA C_{max,ss} and AUC_{0-24,ss} when coadministered with fluconazole was predicted to be 2.1- and 3.2-fold, respectively, in children 6 through 11 years weighing \ge 40 kg or &lt;40 kg.
L2542: - The predicted increases in AUC and C_{max} with CYP3A inhibitors were similar between children aged 6 through 11 years and adults at the proposed therapeutic doses of VNZ and D-IVA, assuming the exposure levels of CYP34 inhibitors are similar between adults and pediatrics.
- PBPK simulations in adults support reduction in the therapeutic dose of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA when coadministered with CYP3A4 inhibitors as follows:When coadministered with a strong CYP3A inhibitor reduce dose from two FDC tablets of (VNZ 10 mg/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) daily to one FDC tablet once weekly (b) (4)
- When coadministered with a moderate CYP3A inhibitor reduce dose from two FDC tablets of (VNZ 10 mg/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) daily to one FDC tablet every other day (b) (4)
- PBPK simulations did not rule out the DDI risk for clinical inhibition of D-IVA with a sensitive CYP2C9 substrate (e.g., warfarin and tolbutamide)
L2543: ## PBPK Model for Tezacaftor
L2544: The PBPK model for TEZ was reviewed under the original NDA 210491. The analysis was considered adequate to predict the extent of the interaction effect with a moderate CYP3A inhibitors under multiple-dose TEZ. Tezacaftor steady-state AUC and C_{max} were predicted to increase 2.1-fold and 1.7-fold, respectively, when the moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor fluconazole is coadministered (NDA 210491, Clinical Pharmacology and Biopharmaceutics Review(s), Section 4). In a clinical DDI study with TEZ and the strong CYP3A4 inhibitor itraconazole, the observed increase in AUC in the presence of itraconazole was 4-fold. The Applicant has prioritized matching VNZ and D-IVA exposures over matching TEZ exposure for dose adjustments to ensure exposures remain within the range of the therapeutic regimen. The reason for this approach is that VNZ and D-IVA exposures were substantially increased by CYP3A inhibitors as compared to TEZ. Therefore, dose adjustment of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA will be by the same percentage. This rationale was considered acceptable by the review team.
L2545: ## PBPK Model for Vanzacaftor
L2546: ## Background
L2547: VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA are mainly metabolized by CYP3A4/5. In recombinant human CYPs system, the remaining percentages of VNZ in CYP3A4 and CYP3A5 incubates were 43% and 81%, respectively, suggesting CYP3A4 and CYP3A5 may be the CYP isozymes involved in VNZ metabolism (Study N295).
L2548: VNZ showed inhibition of CYP2B6 (IC_{50}=14.2 \mu M), CYP2C8 (IC_{50}=18.1 \mu M), and CYP2C9 (IC_{50}=18.4 \mu M) in vitro (Study N189). At clinically relevant exposures, VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA
L2549: and their major metabolites showed low potential for CYP induction in human hepatocyte in vitro assessments using mRNA as endpoint (Study 661-006 and Study CNCT-1005)
L2550: The Madin-Darby canine kidney and Caco-2 cells permeability results indicated VNZ is a low permeable compound (Reports 280 and 327) and D-IVA exhibited moderate permeability in wild-type Madin-Darby canine kidney II cells (Report CTP-656 DM-090). In vitro, VNZ was not a substrate of efflux or hepatic uptake transporters, and it showed low potential to inhibit organic anion transporter (OAT)P1B1 (IC_{50}=45.1 \muM, R =1.008), and OATP1B3 (IC_{50}=61.7 \muM, R =1.006), but it may inhibit breast cancer resistance protein (BCRP) (IC_{50}=0.252 \muM, R =532), P-glycoprotein (P-gp) (IC_{50}=3.8, R =35), and OAT1 (IC_{50}=0.42 \muM, R =0.8) (see Section 8.2). The plasma protein binding, mainly to albumin, for VNZ and D-IVA is &gt;99% (Reports N355, M108, and T235).
L2551: Human mass balance data of VNZ (Study 004) showed that mean AUC_{0-inf} value for unchanged VNZ in plasma was approximately 77% of circulating TRA as determined by the AUC ratio after a single dose of ^{14}C-VNZ 20 mg/200 \muCi administered as an oral solution. Mean recovery of TRA was 0.502% in urine and 91.6% of the radioactive dose recovered in feces, demonstrating minimal renal excretion and major fecal excretion. VNZ accounted for most of the total circulating radioactivity in plasma (mean =96.2%) without major circulating metabolites. VNZ is mainly cleared by metabolism in humans with unchanged VNZ recovered in excreta accounted for less than 10% of the administered radioactive dose. The most abundant metabolite in feces was M6-VNZ, which accounted for 26.1% of the administered radioactive dose followed by M55-VNZ, M19-VNZ, M57-VNZ, and M9-VNZ, which accounted for 7.4%, 6.9%, 6.9%, and 6.8% of the administered dose, respectively. The mean t_{1/2}, clearance (CL)/F and V_z/F of VNZ in plasma were 60.8 hr, 0.596 L/hr and 50.6 L, respectively. The distribution to blood was 56%.
L2552: ## Methods
L2553: ## Software
L2554: Simcyp® Version 21 (Simcyp Ltd, A Certara Company) was used for PBPK analyses by the Applicant and the review team.
L2555: ## Model Development
L2556: The VNZ base model was constructed using the available in vitro ADME data and clinical data (Study 007; itraconazole DDI study). The fraction of VNZ escaping gut extraction (F_g) and fraction of VNZ dose absorbed (F_a) were predicted to be 0.98 and 0.90, respectively, by the first-order absorption model, and bioavailability (F) was estimated to be 0.88. The absorption rate constant (K_a) for VNZ was optimized to 0.91 h^{-1}.
L2557: The central volume of distribution and CL were estimated using the full PBPK model and PK data from Study 007 in which a single dose of 5 mg VNZ Form A was administered alone or in the presence of itraconazole. The fraction of VNZ metabolized by CYP3A4 (f_m CYP3A4) was optimized to 0.95 based on in vitro human CYP reaction phenotyping studies (Study N295, % of VNZ remaining in CYP3A4 occupant was 43%) and the clinical itraconazole DDI data (Study 007, f_m=0.71).
L2558: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L2559: In response to FDA's request for information (dated August 23, 2024), the Applicant conducted sensitivity analysis of CYP3A4 f_m values in the range of 60% to 95% to evaluate the impact of uncertainty of the CYP3A4 f_m value on the DDI effect of CYP3A4 inhibitors (Figure 87). Using a CYP3A4 f_m value of 0.95, the predicted AUC_{last}R value was 3.2 following VNZ administration with and without itraconazole for 14 days, which is at the lower limit of the observed AUC_{last}R (90%CI) of 3.49 (3.22, 3.78). Moreover, CYP3A4 f_m of 0.95 predicted a AUC_{inf}R of 10.5 following VNZ administration with and without itraconazole for 65 days. On the other hand, the AUC ratios of VNZ were relatively insensitive to the CYP3A4 f_m values in the range of 60% to 95% for moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors (e.g., fluconazole). For example, the range of the AUC_{inf}R for VNZ over the considered f_m values was approximately 1.6 (Figure 88).

L2567: CYP3A4-mediated intrinsic clearance, CL_{int}, was calculated using the retrograde method, an f_m=0.95 and estimated hepatic clearance CL_{iv} of 0.52 L/h. Table 161 summarizes model input parameters for VNZ.
L2568: The predicted V_{ss} was 0.605 (L/kg) using Method 2 and a full PBPK model which is comparable to the measured V_{ss}=50.6 L from the mass balance Study 004 (0.583 L/kg for subjects weighting 86.8 kg) and to population pharmacokinetic (PopPK) predicted volume of distribution/F =59.2 L for Study 007. The mean CL/F of unchanged VNZ obtained from the mass balance Study 004 was 0.596 L/h. The apparent clearance (CL/F =0.78 L/h) obtained from the PBPK predicted VNZ PK profiles when administered alone is comparable to the CL/F =0.761 obtained from Study 007. The PBPK predicted oral CL (Dose/AUC_{inf}) of VNZ PK profile when administered with itraconazole was 0.13 L/h, while the observed CL/F in Study 007 was 0.124 L/h. The blood to plasma ratio of 0.588 is comparable to the distribution to blood of 56% obtained from the mass balance Study 004.

L2575: Based on in vitro studies, VNZ is not a substrate of P-gp, BCRP, OATP1B1, or OATP1B3. VNZ may have the potential to inhibit BCRP (IC_{50}=0.252 \mu M, R =532), P-gp (IC_{50}=3.8 \mu M, R =35) and OAT1 (IC_{50}=0.42 \mu M, R =0.8). The VNZ inhibition parameters towards CYP2C9 (IC_{50}=18.4 \mu M) determined in vitro (Study N189) was used in the model for DDI risk assessment on CYP2C9 substrates (e.g., warfarin and tolbutamide).
L2576: ## Model Validation
L2577: Clinical PK and DDI studies used to support VNZ model development, and validation are listed in Table 162.
L2578: Vanzacaftor was provided in two forms in the clinical program, Form A and Form D. Form D has had 50% bioavailability of Form A based on relative bioavailability results. To simulate the PK profiles of VNZ Form D that was used in phase 3 studies in FDC, half the clinical dose was used during simulations (i.e., if the clinical dose of VNZ is 20 mg, simulations were conducted with 10 mg dose to capture the PK profile of 20 mg).
L2579: In response to FDA's request for information (dated August 23, 2024), the Applicant conducted additional validation of the predictive performance of the PBPK models of VNZ and D-IVA (and its metabolites) for multiple-dose PK in subjects with cystic fibrosis using FDC tablet from Studies 102 and 103 (Table 162). Additional validation for VNZ PBPK model was performed by the review team using PK data obtained following the administration of single or multiple doses of VNZ Form A or Form D.
L2580: All simulations were conducted in adult healthy population with VNZ administered in fed state. The fixed-dose combination drug is recommended to be taken with food. In DDI simulations with itraconazole, VNZ was administered at the same time in fed state. Simulations conducted with VNZ administered in fed state 1 hour following the administration of itraconazole in fasted state showed similar PK parameters compared to when VNZ and itraconazole were administered at the same time.

L2588: ## Perpetrator Models
L2589: The software's default PBPK models of the strong CYP3A inhibitor itraconazole (fasted solution) and moderate CYP3A inhibitors fluconazole, erythromycin, and verapamil, were used to predict the CYP3A inhibition effects on VNZ PK.
L2590: ## Model Application
L2591: - To simulate the effect of the strong CYP3A4 inhibitor itraconazole on the PK of VNZ at steady state in adults and children aged 6 through 11 years.
- To simulate the effect of moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors (fluconazole, erythromycin, and verapamil) on the PK of VNZ following single-dose and at steady state, in adults and children aged 6 through 11 years.
- To determine dose adjustment regimens when using the FDC tablet (VNZ Form D 10 mg/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) in the presence of strong and moderate CYP3A inhibitors in adults and children 6 through 11 years of age.
L2592: ## Results
L2593: ## Model Validation
L2594: ## *Evaluation of the Predictive Performance for PK Under Different Dosing Scenario*
L2595: The VNZ PBPK model adequately described the VNZ plasma concentration-time profiles (Figure 89) and PK parameters (AUC_{last} and C_{max}) following a single dose of 5 mg [Form A] administered under fed condition with predicted/observed ratios within 0.8 to 1.25 (Table 163). Further, the model adequately predicted the PK of VNZ in the presence of itraconazole up to 264 hours post VNZ coadministration (Figure 89), and the AUC_{last} and C_{max} ratio for the DDI with itraconazole (Table 164).
L2596: Of note, in the clinical DDI study (Study 007), itraconazole (solution) was taken in the fasted condition approximately 1 hour before VNZ dosing in the fed state on the day of coadministration. Although the Applicant's PBPK analyses allowed dosing of VNZ under fed state and itraconazole under fasted state, the 1-hour staggering in drugs dosing was not applied. The review team conducted simulations between VNZ and itraconazole with 1-hour staggering between the two drugs, and the simulated PK profiles and DDI effects were similar to dosing VNZ and itraconazole at the same time. Moreover, this reviewer conducted the simulation with itraconazole administered on Day 15 and VNZ administered on Day 18 (after 72 hours) and the results were comparable to same day administration.
L2597: Additional model validation was conducted using PK of VNZ administered as 10 mg (Form A, separate tablet, Study 005) and 20 mg (Form A separate tablet, Study 003), which showed
L2598: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacafter/tezacafter/deutivacafter) Tablets
L2599: predicted/observed ratios for AUC_{last} and C_{max} within 0.8 to 1.25 (Table 163, Figure 90). Moreover, the review team conducted model validation using multiple dose PK data in healthy subjects of VNZ from two cohorts in Study 006. The predicted/observed ratios for AUC_{last} and C_{max} were within 0.8 to 1.25 for one cohort in Study 006 (Table 163).
L2600: It appears that the performance of VNZ PBPK model to predict the PK data obtained from healthy subjects (e.g., Studies 007, 005, and 006) is different to certain extent from the predictability of PK data from subjects with CF (e.g., Studies 102 and 103). Of note, VNZ model prediction of PK data from subjects with CF is around unity, compared to a wide prediction performance (e.g., predicted/observed ratios range from 0.8 to 1.32) using PK data from healthy subjects.

L2623: ## Model Application
L2624: ## *Predict DDI With Single Dose and The proposed Therapeutic Dose of VNZ With CYP3A Inhibitors and Inducers*
L2625: VNZ PBPK model was used to simulate the effect of multiple doses of strong and moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors on the PK of single and multiple doses of VNZ. Simulation results predicted 10.5-fold increase in the AUC_{inf} and a small change was predicted in C_{max} (1.2-fold) of VNZ following the administration of 5 mg single dose with multiple doses of itraconazole (Table 165). Further, AUC_{inf} of VNZ was predicted to increase by approximately 2.4- to 3.9-fold with minimal change in C_{max} (1.2-fold) when VNZ is coadministered as a single dose with multiple doses of a moderate CYP3A inhibitor (Table 165).
L2626: The Applicant proposed to use the predicted exposure change after single dose administration of VNZ to determine the DDI dose adjustment with CYP3A inhibitors. In response to FDA's request for information (dated August 23, 2024), the Applicant conducted PBPK simulations to predict AUC_{0-24h,ss} and C_{max,ss} ratios for the proposed therapeutic doses of VNZ (20 mg, Form D,
L2627: once daily for 40 weeks) at steady state in the presence of strong and moderate CYP3A inhibitors. Due to VNZ concentration accumulation after multiple doses, the predicted ratios of C_{max,ss} at steady state were higher than the ratios of C_{max} after a single dose. The predicted ratios of AUC_{0-24h,ss} at steady state for VNZ with and without multiple doses of CYP3A inhibitors were similar to those from the single dose simulations based on AUC_{inf}. Based on these results, the performance of VNZ PBPK model to predict DDI appears compatible when VNZ was administered as a single dose and at the proposed therapeutic doses. Therefore, the predicted AUC ratios after single dose administration of VNZ can be used to determine dose adjustment regimens when using the FDC tablet (VNZ Form D 10 mg /TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) in the presence of strong and moderate CYP3A inhibitors.

L2636: ## *Comparison of Predicted DDI Ratios With CYP3A4 Inhibitors Between Adult and Pediatric (6 through 11 years) Populations at the Proposed Therapeutic Doses of VNZ*
L2637: The exposure changes of VNZ when coadministered with strong or moderate CYP3A inhibitors in adults were used to determine the DDI dose adjustment in children 6 through 11 years of age. This approach assumes the effect of these CYP3A inhibitors on the PK of VNZ in this pediatric age group are comparable to those in adults. To support this assumption, the pediatric population model should be able to describe the PK of VNZ in children and the predicted DDI ratios with CYP3A inhibitors in adults and pediatrics should be similar.
L2638: In response to FDA's request for information (dated August 23, 2024), the Applicant used the PK data of VNZ from Trial 105 to demonstrate the predictive PK performance of the pediatric
L2639: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L2640: PBPK model for children 6 through 11 years of age, categorized by body weight (&lt;40 kg or ≥40 kg). Pediatric subjects with cystic fibrosis in Cohort B1 from Trial 105 received a weight-based dosing of the triple combination (TC) as follows:
L2641: - For pediatric &lt;40 kg, the TC was given as VNZ 12 mg [Form D] /TEZ 60 mg/D-IVA 150 mg administrated once daily for 24 weeks.
- For pediatric ≥40 kg, the TC was given as VNZ 20 mg [Form D] /TEZ 100 mg/D-IVA 250 mg administrated once daily for 24 weeks.
L2642: The pediatric model adequately described VNZ plasma concentration-time profiles from Trial 105 (Figure 91) and PK parameters (AUC_{0-24,ss} and C_{max,ss}) with predicted/observed ratios marginally at acceptable limit of 0.8 to 1.25 for pediatrics ≥40 kg, while the ratios for pediatrics &lt;40 kg were considerably above the acceptable limit (Table 166). Although the observed PK data from Trial 105 were overpredicted by the PBPK model in pediatric subjects &lt;40 kg, most of the observed data is contained between the 5th and 95th percentile of the simulated PK profile of VNZ in pediatric 6 through 11 years weighing &lt;40 kg (Figure 91). Overall, the comparison of the observed and predicted pediatric steady-state PK parameters of VNZ appears reasonable due to the limited PK data of VNZ in pediatric subjects at the time of NDA submission in addition to the high variability observed in the PK data from Trial 105.

L2652: Afterward, the Applicant used the pediatric PBPK model to predict the effects of coadministration of strong and moderate CYP3A inhibitors on the PK of VNZ in children aged 6 through 11 years. A comparison of the predicted ratios of AUC_{0-24,ss} and C_{max,ss} for VNZ after multiple daily doses when coadministered with the strong CYP3A inhibitor itraconazole and the moderate CYP3A inhibitor fluconazole in this pediatric group and in adults is provided in Table 167. Note that the doses of the CYP3A inhibitors were reduced in the pediatric group to match the exposure levels observed in healthy adult subjects at steady state. Overall, the fold changes in AUC and C_{max} with CYP3A inhibitors were similar between pediatric 6 through 11 years and adult populations at the proposed therapeutic doses of VNZ, assuming the exposure levels of the CYP3A inhibitor are similar between adult and pediatric populations.

L2663: ## PBPK Model for D-IVA and Metabolites
L2664: ## Method
L2665: ## Software
L2666: Simcyp® Version 21 (Simcyp Ltd, A Certara Company) was used for PBPK analyses by the Applicant and the review team.
L2667: ## Model Development
L2668: ## D-IVA
L2669: The D-IVA PBPK model consists of a first-order absorption model constructed using available in vitro ADME data and drug interaction with itraconazole (Study VX18-445-006, NDA 212273). The absorption of D-IVA was assumed to be complete (F_a=1), and the absorption rate constant for D-IVA was assumed to be similar to ivacaftor (K_a=0.546 h^{-1}). (See NDA 203188, Clinical Pharmacology and Biopharmaceutics Review(s), Appendix 1). The F_g of D-IVA was predicted to be 0.76.
L2670: D-IVA PK was described by a one-compartment popPK model with seven transit compartments, first-order absorption, and linear elimination (Report t406). A minimal PBPK model and single adjusting compartment were used to estimate V_{ss}=0.85 L/kg for D-IVA with K_{in} and K_{out} of 0.1 and 0.14 h^{-1}, respectively.
L2671: Ivacaftor has a large V_z/F, suggesting extensive tissue distribution. The mean V_z/F of ivacaftor after a single dose of 275 mg of ivacaftor in healthy subjects and subjects with CF in fed state
L2672: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L2673: was 203 to 206 L and after multiple-dose administration of ivacaftor 250 mg q12h was 206 L in healthy subjects in the fed state. Similarly, D-IVA showed large V_z/F. Values of 106 L and 250 L were reported when coadministered with and without itraconazole, respectively (Study VX18-445-006), and a V_z/F = 183 L was estimated by popPK analysis (Report t406). The geometric mean predicted V_{ss} for D-IVA when administered alone was 228 L calculated from CL and \Lambda_z, which is comparable to the observed value in Study VX18-445-006. The predicted geometric mean CL of D-IVA when coadministered with and without itraconazole was 11.83 L/h and 1.53 L/h, respectively, which is comparable to the observed CL values of 11.9 L/h and 1.01 L/h, respectively, from Study VX18-445-006.
L2674: The fraction of D-IVA metabolized by CYP3A4 was optimized to 0.95 based on in vitro human CYP reaction phenotyping studies (Report S286, % of D-IVA remaining in CYP3A4 occupant was 6%) and the clinical itraconazole DDI data (Study VX18-445-006, f_m = 0.9). Sensitivity analysis of CYP3A4 f_m showed that there is a narrow range of CYP3A4 f_m values that is required to describe the clinical DDI data with itraconazole for D-IVA. For example, using CYP3A4 f_m of 0.95 predicted an AUC_{infR} = 12.4 following D-IVA administration with and without itraconazole that was contained within the observed 90%CI of AUC_{infR} (8.72 – 14.1) (Figure 92). The AUC_{infR} for D-IVA was relatively insensitive to CYP3A4 f_m values in the range of 60% to 95% for fluconazole. For example, the range of AUC_{infR} for D-IVA over the considered f_m values was approximately 1.8 to 2.9 for the moderate CYP3A inhibitor fluconazole (Figure 92).
L2675: The CL_{int} of 24.2 \mu L/min/pmol for D-IVA by CYP3A was calculated using the retrograde method based on estimated CL_{iv} = 7.1 L/h and f_m = 0.95. The additional clearance by human liver microsomes was estimated to be 155.7 \mu L/min/mg protein. The PBPK model input parameters for D-IVA are listed in Table 168.

L2683: The D-IVA inhibitory potential of CYP enzymes were included in the model. This is because in vitro data showed that D-IVA inhibited CYP2C8 (IC_{50}=9.5 \muM), CYP2C9 (IC_{50}=15.6 \muM), and CYP3A4 (IC_{50}=15.7 \muM, testosterone as a probe substrate) with R-value above the defined criteria (Reports D-IVA DM-058, CPT-656-DM-085). Clinically, IVA did not significantly affect the PK of rosiglitazone, a CYP2C8 substrate, and IVA and TEZ/IVA had no clinically significant effect on midazolam exposures, CYP3A substrate (Study 770-010, NDA 203188 and Study 661-006, NDA 210491). Based on in vitro inhibition parameters and the above clinical DDI data, it is expected that D-IVA and its metabolites have low potential to mediate CYP enzymes inhibition at clinically relevant concentrations, except that D-IVA may inhibit CYP2C9.
L2684: There is no mass balance Study available for D-IVA. In mass balance Study 003 for ivacaftor (NDA 203188), mean total recovery was 94.6%, of which 87.8% (mean) was excreted in feces and 6.6% was excreted in urine. The amount of unchanged drug excreted in feces was only 2.52%, suggesting that most of the drug was excreted as metabolites.
L2685: Based on in vitro studies, D-IVA and M1-D-IVA are substrates of P-gp and BCRP. The model did not include intestinal efflux transport-mediated disposition for D-IVA based on the assumption of high oral absorption (as reported with ivacaftor) and approximately linear PK after single dose. D-IVA and M1-D-IVA are P-gp inhibitors with IC_{50} of 3.83 and 9.86 \muM,
L2686: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L2687: respectively (see Section 8.2). However, when TEZ/IVA was coadministered with digoxin, Study 661-006 (NDA 210491), a sensitive P-gp substrate, digoxin exposure increased by 1.3-fold, indicating weak inhibition of P-gp by IVA (IC_{50}=10.6 \muM) which has similar P-gp inhibition potency as D-IVA. Therefore, the DDI risk of D-IVA is predicted to be similar to IVA. Moreover, D-IVA and M1-D-IVA are BCRP inhibitors with IC_{50} of 1.19 and 9.15 \muM, respectively (see Section 8.2).
L2688: ## Model Validation
L2689: Clinical PK and DDI studies used to support the model development and validation are listed in Table 169. The PBPK models for D-IVA and its metabolites were developed and validated using the PK data obtained from DDI with itraconazole (Study VX18-445-006, NDA 212273).
L2690: Study VX18-445-006 evaluated the effect of itraconazole on the PK of a single dose of elaxaftor (ELX) 20 mg/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 50 mg and relevant metabolites administered as separate tablets. In period 1, ELX, TEZ, and D-IVA, were administered on Day 1 in fed state followed by 14 to 21 washout period. In Period 2 (Day -4 to 7), itraconazole 200 mg was administered in fasted from Day -3 to Day 6, followed by breakfast and administration of ELX/TEZ/D-IVA on Day 1 (i.e., Day 4 of itraconazole administration).
L2691: All PBPK simulation were conducted in fed state with ELX/TEZ/D-IVA administered on Day 4 of itraconazole administration with 1 hours staggering where itraconazole was administered in the fasted state.

L2698: ## Perpetrator Models
L2699: The software's default PBPK models of the strong CYP3A inhibitor itraconazole (fasted solution) and moderate CYP3A inhibitors fluconazole, erythromycin, and verapamil were used to predict the CYP3A inhibition effects on the PK of D-IVA.
L2700: ## Model Application
L2701: - To simulate the effect of moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors (fluconazole, erythromycin, and verapamil) on the PK of D-IVA in adults and children aged 6 through 11 years.
- To determine dose adjustment regimens when using the FDC tablet (VNZ Form D 10 mg /TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) in the presence of strong and moderate CYP3A inhibitors in adults and children aged 6 through 11 years.

L2723: In response to FDA's request for information (dated August 23, 2024), the Applicant conducted additional validation of D-IVA PBPK model by comparing predicted versus observed steady-state PK parameters of D-IVA, M1-D-IVA, and M6-D-IVA in CF subjects from studies 102 and 103 when D-IVA was administered as FDC tablet (TEZ 100 mg/VNZ 20 mg Form D/D-IVA 250 mg) once daily in fed state (Table 172 and Figure 94). It appears that D-IVA model tends to under predict the popPK estimates of AUC_{last} from studies 102 and 103 following the administration of 250 mg once daily. Similarly, the additional model validation, conducted by the reviewer, showed that D-IVA model under predicted the observed AUC_{last} from Study 006. This bias for under prediction noted in the D-IVA model could be justified by the high variability in observed and predicted PK data.
L2724: The review team acknowledges this limitation in the D-IVA PBPK model. We note, however, this model limitation is not expected to have a significant impact on the overall application to inform dose reduction when D-IVA is coadministered with CYP3A4 inhibitors, when considering the constraints of DDI dose adjustment strategy (i.e., use of FDC formulation, reducing number of tablets and/or dosing frequency).

L2739: ## Model Application
L2740: ## *Predict DDI With Single Dose and the Proposed Therapeutic Dose of D-IVA With CYP3A Inhibitors and Inducers*
L2741: The D-IVA model was used to simulate the effect of multiple doses of strong and moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors on the PK of single (50 mg) and multiple doses (250 mg once daily for 12 weeks) of D-IVA. Simulation results predicted 12.4-fold increase in the AUC_{inf}, and 1.9-fold increase in C_{max} of D-IVA following the administration of a single 50 mg dose of D-IVA with itraconazole. Further, simulations predicted 2.9- to 4.78-fold increase in AUC_{inf} of D-IVA when D-IVA was coadministered as a single dose with moderate CYP3A inhibitors (Table 173).
L2742: PBPK simulations to predict AUC_{0-24h,ss} ratios for the proposed therapeutic doses of D-IVA (250 mg once daily) at steady state in the presence of strong or moderate CYP3A inhibitors showed similar increase in AUC_{0-24h,ss} of D-IVA when compared to DDI ratios of single dose D-IVA. Due to D-IVA concentration accumulation after multiple doses, the predicted ratios of C_{max,ss} at steady state were higher than the ratios of C_{max} after a single dose (Table 173). The predicted ratios of AUC_{0-24h,ss} at steady state for D-IVA with and without multiple doses of CYP3A inhibitor were similar to those from the single dose simulations based on AUC_{inf}.

L2750: ## *Comparison of Predicted DDI Ratios With CYP3A4 Inhibitors Between Adult and Pediatric (6 Through 11 Years) Populations at the Proposed Therapeutic Doses of D-IVA*
L2751: Similar to analysis conducted for VNZ, the Applicant used the PK data of D-IVA from Trial 105 to demonstrate the predictive PK performance of the pediatric PBPK model for children 6 through 11 years of age, categorized by body weight (&lt;40 kg or ≥40 kg).
L2752: The predicted PK parameters (AUC_{0-24,ss} and C_{max,ss}) for D-IVA and its metabolites were within a ~2-fold range of the observed PK data from Trial 105 Cohort B1 due to the high variability in PK data (Table 174). Generally, the observed data is contained between the 5th and 95th percentile of the simulated PK profile of D-IVA in pediatric 6 through 11 years weighing &lt;40 kg; however, the model under predicted the PK profiles of D-IVA in pediatric 6 through 11 years weighing ≥40 kg (Figure 95). Overall, the comparison of the observed and predicted pediatric steady-state PK parameters of D-IVA appears reasonable due to the limited PK data of D-IVA in pediatric subjects at the time of NDA submission and the limitation noted in D-IVA PBPK model, as previously discussed.

L2777: ## **PBPK Modeling to Support Dose Adjustment of VNZ and D-IVA**
L2778: Coadministration of TC therapy with CYP3A4 inhibitors resulted in a substantial decrease in CL/F which in return increased the overall exposure of VNZ and D-IVA compared to subjects taking TC therapy without coadministration of CYP3A4 inhibitors. PBPK simulations were performed to evaluate two different dose adjustment regimens of VNZ and D-IVA when the FDC tablet (VNZ Form D 10 mg /TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) is coadministered with strong or moderate CYP3A inhibitors in adults. Given that the predicted increase exposures in the presence of CYP3A inhibitors were higher for VNZ and D-IVA compared to TEZ, and the constraints of using a FDC tablet, the Applicant has prioritized matching VNZ and D-IVA exposures over matching TEZ exposure for DDI dose adjustments to ensure exposures remain within the range of the therapeutic regimen. Therefore, dose adjustment of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA will be by the same percentage.
L2779: ## **Strong CYP3A Inhibitors**
L2780: The Applicant has evaluated two dose adjustments, one FDC tablet once weekly and two FDC tablets every other week, against the exposures at the therapeutic dose in the absence of CYP3A inhibitors, two FDC tablets once daily. The simulated plasma concentration-time profiles of VNZ and D-IVA are shown in Figure 96. The geometric fold change of simulated steady-state VNZ and D-IVA exposures for the dose adjustment regimens in the presence of a strong CYP3A inhibitor (itraconazole) relative to two FDC tablets once daily in the absence of a CYP3A inhibitor are presented in Table 176. Alternative dosing regimens of VNZ (one FDC Q1W and two FDC Q2W) coadministered with strong CYP3A4 inhibitors has maintained exposure within or below the 5th and 95th percentiles determined from therapeutic dosing regimen of TC (two FDC QD) in the absence of strong CYP3A3 inhibitors. The exposure to VNZ and D-IVA from the two dose adjustment regimes in the presence of strong CYP3A4 inhibitors is within 1.5-fold of VNZ and D-IVA exposures from the therapeutic dosing regimen in the absence of strong CYP3A4 inhibitors (Table 176).
L2781: ## **Moderate CYP3A4 Inhibitors**
L2782: Two dose adjustments, one FDC tablet every other day and two FDC tablets on Day 1 and two FDC tablets on Day 4 weekly, against the exposures at the therapeutic dose in the absence of
L2783: CYP3A inhibitors, two FDC tablets once daily. The simulated plasma concentration-time profiles of VNZ and D-IVA are shown in Figure 97 and Figure 98, and the geometric fold change of simulated steady-state VNZ and D-IVA exposures for the dose adjustment regimens in the presence of moderate CYP3A inhibitors (fluconazole, erythromycin, and verapamil) relative to two FDC tablets once daily in the absence of a CYP3A inhibitor are presented in Table 176. Alternative dosing regimens of VNZ and D-IVA coadministered with moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors has maintained exposure within or below the 5th and 95th percentiles determined from therapeutic dosing regimen in the absence of moderate CYP3A3 inhibitors. The exposure to VNZ and D-IVA from the two dose adjustment regimes (1FDC Q2D and 4 FDC D1D4) in the presence of moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors is within 1.5-fold of VNZ or D-IVA exposure from the therapeutic dosing regimen 2FDC QD in the absence of moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors.

L2810: PopPK model-based simulations were performed to inform dose adjustments for adults and children aged 6 through 11 years with CF that were coadministered CYP3A4 inhibitors. For these simulations, clearance values were adjusted for subjects receiving strong or moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor based on changes in AUC predicted by the PBPK simulations (Table 165 and Table 173). The potential dose adjustments for children aged 6 to 11 years coadministered VNZ and D-IVA with CYP3A4 inhibitors aimed to provide exposures that are similar to VNZ and D-IVA exposures without CYP3A4 inhibitors in adults with CF. Review of this model-based analysis and final recommendations of DDI dose adjustment for pediatrics are presented in Section 14.5.1 of the review.
L2811: ## **Prediction of DDI Potential of VNZ and D-IVA as Victims for Moderate and Strong CYP3A4 Inducers**
L2812: In response to FDA's information request (dated September 6, 2024), the Applicant provided PBPK simulations to support the DDI management strategy of VNZ/D-IVA with moderate and strong CYP3A inducers in the proposed label. Simulation showed that the exposure to VNZ and D-IVA decreased significantly by 82% and 90%, respectively, when coadministered with rifampin, a strong CYP3A inducer. Moreover, carbamazepine, a strong CYP3A inducer, significantly decreased VNZ and D-IVA exposures by 57% and 76%, respectively (Table 177).
L2813: Simulation showed that the exposure to VNZ and D-IVA decreased by 69% and 73%, respectively, when coadministered with efavirenz, a moderate CYP3A inducer.
L2814: In summary, moderate or strong CYP3A4 inducers significantly decreased the exposure to VNZ and D-IVA, which may impact the efficacy of TC therapy in CF patients. Therefore, coadministration of TC therapy with moderate or strong CYP3A4 inducers is not recommended.

L2822: ## **Prediction of DDI Potential of VNZ and D-IVA as Inhibitors on CYP2C9 Substrates**
L2823: In vitro data showed that D-IVA inhibited CYP2C8 (IC50=9.5 µM, R-value =1.05), CYP2C9 (IC50=15.6 µM, R-value =1.03), and CYP3A4 (IC50=15.7 µM, testosterone as a probe substrate, R-value =1.03) with R-value above the defined criteria (Reports D-IVA DM-058, CPT-656-DM-085). Clinically, IVA did not significantly affect the PK of rosiglitazone, a CYP2C8 substrate (Study 770-010, NDA 203188) or midazolam, CYP3A substrate, (Study 661-006, NDA 210491)). Moreover, in vitro data showed that M1-D-IVA inhibited CYP2C9 (IC50=4.6 µM, R-value =1.09). Therefore, D-IVA and its metabolites have low potential to mediate CYP enzymes inhibition at clinically relevant concentrations, except that D-IVA and M1-D-IVA may inhibit
L2824: CYP2C9. VNZ may have weak inhibition CYP2C9 (IC_{50}=18.4 \muM) (Study N189) with R-value below the defined criteria.
L2825: In response to FDA's information request (dated September 6, 2024), The Applicant provided PBPK simulations for DDI of multiple-doses of VNZ and D-IVA as perpetrators (i.e., CYP2C9 inhibitors) with a CYP2C9 substrate (e.g., warfarin and tolbutamide). Using in vitro determined IC_{50} values, the inhibition of CYP2C9 by VNZ or D-IVA had no notable impact on the exposure to warfarin and tolbutamide (Table 178). However, applying a minimal uncertainty factor of 3-fold lower IC_{50} value for D-IVA, the risk for a clinical interaction could not be excluded (e.g., the predicted AUC R values of tolbutamide reached the threshold of 1.25). Therefore, the PBPK analysis supports the Applicant's proposed DDI management strategy of cautionary use or monitoring when D-IVA is coadministered with CYP2C9 substrates, such as warfarin.

L2830: The PBPK model for VNZ included the inhibition potential CYP1A2 (IC_{50}=36.4 \muM), CYP2B6 (IC_{50}=14.2 \muM), CYP2C8 (IC_{50}=18.1 \muM), CYP2C9 (IC_{50}=18.4 \muM), CYP2C19 (IC_{50}=51 \muM), CYP2D6 (IC_{50}=30.9 \muM), and CYP3A4 (IC_{50}=33 \muM) and unfound fraction in the microsomes (f_{u,mic}) of 0.00709 (Report N189). The PBPK model for D-IVA included the inhibition potential CYP2C8 (IC_{50}=9.5 \muM), CYP2C9 (IC_{50}=15.6 \muM), and CYP3A4 (IC_{50}=15.7 \muM) and unfound fraction in the microsomes (f_{u,mic}) of 0.0196 (Report D-IVA DM-058). The PBPK model for M1-D-IVA included the inhibition potential CYP2B6 (IC_{50}=23.2 \muM), CYP2C8 (IC_{50}=4.8 \muM), CYP2C9 (IC_{50}=4.6 \muM), CYP2C19 (IC_{50}=46 \muM), CYP2D6 (IC_{50}=55.9 \muM), and CYP3A4 (IC_{50}=14.9 \muM, midazolam as probe substrate) and unfound fraction in the microsomes (f_{u,mic}) of 0.167 (Report CTP-656 DM-085).
L2831: The in vitro CYP2C9 IC_{50} values for VNZ, D-IVA, and M1-D-IVA measured in human liver microsomes were corrected for nonspecific binding in the assay to obtain free IC_{50} values (Reports DM-058, CTP-656 DM-085, S016, R015, and S079). The free IC_{50} values were varied 3-fold from the measured value in both directions to incorporate the impact of any experimental uncertainty in measurements.
L2832: a The PBPK model included D-IVA and M1-D-IVA but not M6-D-IVA since Simcyp software limitations only allow parent and a single metabolite to be included as an inhibitor. However, M6-D-IVA does not inhibit CYP2C9 in vitro and its absence is the model has no impact on the outcome (Report CTP-656 DM-085).
L2833: b CYP2C9 Substrate + CYP2C9 perpetrator/CYP2C9 Substrate. Source: Applicant's response to IR submitted on Sep 6, 2024, SDN 0022, Module 1.11.3, Table 3.
L2834: Abbreviations: AUC0-24h,ss, area under the concentration-time curve from time 0 to 24 hour at steady-state; Cmax, maximum plasma concentration at steady-state; Cmin, minimum plasma concentration at steady-state; CYP, cytochrome P450; D-IVA, deutivacaftor; IC_{50}, half maximal inhibitory concentration; M, metabolite; PBPK, population-based pharmacokinetics QD, once daily; VNZ, vanzacaftor
L2835: ## Conclusions
L2836: VNZ AUC was predicted to increase by approximately 2.4- to 3.9-fold when single or multiple doses of VNZ was coadministered with multiple doses of a moderate CYP3A inhibitor.
L2837: Coadministration of a moderate CYP3A inhibitor with single dose VNZ increased VNZ C_{max} by 1.2-fold, whereas VNZ C_{max,ss} increased by 2.5- to 3.8-fold due to accumulation.
L2838: Simulations predicted 2.9- to 4.5-fold increase in AUC_{inf} and 3.1- to 5.1-fold increase in D-IVA AUC_{0-24,ss} when D-IVA was coadministered as a single dose or multiple doses, respectively, with a moderate CYP3A inhibitor. Coadministration of a moderate CYP3A inhibitor with single dose D-IVA increased D-IVA C_{max} by 1.7-fold, whereas D-IVA C_{max,ss} increased by 2.3 to 3.4-fold due to accumulation.
L2839: Simulation predicted increase in VNZ C_{max,ss} and AUC_{0-24,ss} by approximately 10- and 11-fold in children 6 through 11 years weighing \ge 40 kg, and 10- and 9.5-fold in children 6 through 11 years weighing &lt;40 kg, respectively, when multiple doses of VNZ were coadministered with a strong CYP3A inhibitor. D-IVA geometric mean ratio of C_{max,ss} and AUC_{0-24,ss} by 7.36 and 14.3 (in children 6 through 11 years weighing \ge 40 kg) and 6.76 and 14 (in children 6 through 11 years weighing &lt;40 kg), respectively, when D-IVA was coadministered with itraconazole
L2840: Simulation predicted increase in VNZ geometric mean ratio of C_{max,ss} and AUC_{0-24,ss} by 2.54 and 2.64 (in children 6 through 11 years weighing \ge 40 kg) and 2.49 and 2.6 (in children 6 through 11 years weighing &lt;40 kg) when multiple doses of VNZ were coadministered with fluconazole, a strong CYP3A4. The increase in D-IVA geometric mean ratio of C_{max,ss} and AUC_{0-24,ss} when coadministered with fluconazole was predicted to be 2.1 and 3.17, respectively, in children 6 through 11 years weighing \ge 40 kg or &lt;40 kg.
L2841: PBPK simulations in adults support reduction in the therapeutic dose of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA when coadministered with CYP3A4 inhibitors as follows:
L2842: - When coadministered with a strong CYP3A inhibitor: reduce dose from two FDC tablets of (VNZ 10 mg/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) daily to one FDC tablet once weekly (b) (4)
- When coadministered with a moderate CYP3A inhibitor: reduce dose from two FDC tablets of (VNZ 10 mg/TEZ 50 mg/D-IVA 125 mg) daily to one FDC tablet every other day (b) (4)
L2843: Simulations predicted 82% and 90% reduction in VNZ and D-IVA exposure, respectively, when coadministered with rifampin, a strong CYP3A inducer. In addition, simulations predicted 57% and 76%, reduction in VNZ and D-IVA exposure, respectively, when coadministered with carbamazepine, a strong CYP3A inducer. Efavirenz, a moderate CYP3A inducer, was predicted to decrease the exposure to VNZ and D-IVA by 69% and 73%, respectively. Therefore, simulations support that coadministration of TC therapy with moderate or strong CYP3A4 inducers is not recommended.
L2844: The DDI potential of D-IVA as inhibitor of CYP2C9 substrates (e.g., warfarin and tolbutamide) cannot be ruled out.
L2845: ## 14.6. Pharmacogenetics
L2846: Not applicable.
L2847: ## 15. Study/Trial Design
L2848: ## 15.1. Phase 3 Trials, Trials 102 and 103
L2849: ## Overview and Objectives
L2850: Trials VX20-121-102 (Trial 102) and VX20-121-103 (Trial 103) were phase 3, multicenter, randomized, double-blind, parallel-group, controlled, noninferiority trials evaluating the safety and efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA in subjects with CF 12 years of age and older.
L2851: Trial 102 Title: A Phase 3, Randomized, Double-blind, Controlled Study Evaluating the Efficacy and Safety of VX-121 [VNZ] Combination Therapy [VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA] in Subjects with Cystic Fibrosis who are Heterozygous for *F508del* and a Minimal Function Mutation (F/MF)
L2852: - Trial dates: September 14, 2021 to November 21, 2023
- Trial report date: April 5, 2024
L2853: Trial 103 Title: A Phase 3, Randomized, Double-blind, Controlled Study Evaluating the Efficacy and Safety of VNZ Combination Therapy [VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA] in Subjects with Cystic Fibrosis Who Are Homozygous for *F508del*, Heterozygous for *F508del* and a (F/G) or (F/RF) Mutation, or Have At Least 1 Other Triple Combination Responsive *CFTR* Mutation and No *F508del* Mutation
L2854: - Trial dates: October 27, 2021 to November 30, 2023
- Trial report date: April 5, 2024
L2855: Overall, the two phase 3 trials were similar in design and were adequate and well controlled. The general trial designs will be reviewed together with relevant differences noted.
L2856: ## Primary Objective
L2857: - To evaluate the efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA in CF subjects
L2858: ## Secondary Objectives
L2859: - To evaluate the safety of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA
- To evaluate the pharmacokinetics (PK) of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA
L2860: ## Trial Design
L2861: The phase 3 trials included a 4-week (28 day) screening period followed by a 4-week (28 day) run-in period during which all subjects received ELX 200 mg QD/TEZ 100 mg QD/IVA 150 mg q12h (the commercially approved dose) to establish a reliable on-treatment (ELX/TEZ/IVA) baseline for the treatment period. Spirometry and SwCl assessments obtained during the run-in period (Day -14) were obtained and used for stratification and randomization. Following completion of the run-in period, the subjects were randomized 1:1 to the VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA
L2862: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L2863: (treatment) arm or the ELX/TEZ/IVA (control) arm for a 52-week treatment period that included in-person clinic visits on Day 14, Weeks 4, 8, 12, 16, 24, 36, and 52 with intermittent telephone contact in between scheduled visits. A follow-up safety visit occurred for any subject who prematurely discontinued trial drug (28\pm 7 days after the last dose of trial drug). Safety follow-up visits were not required for subjects who completed the treatment period and transitioned within 28 days to one of the following: an open-label trial, another qualified trial run by the Applicant, or a commercially available or managed access program-supplied program for another medication from the Applicant. The trial schematic is shown below in Figure 99.
L2864: **Figure 99. Pivotal Phase 3 Trial Schematic, Trial 102**
L2865: Source: Applicant provided (Protocol VX20-121-102, Version 3.0. Figure 2-1).
Abbreviations: D-IVA, deutivacaftor; ELX, elaxacaftor; IVA, ivacaftor; N, number of subjects, TEZ, tezacaftor; VX-121 [VNZ], vanzacaftor
L2866: Subjects were permitted to be rescreened once during which all screening assessments were repeated with the exception of *CFTR*-genotyping and, if applicable, follicle-stimulating hormone level and ophthalmologic examinations. Subjects were stratified by age at screening visit (&lt;18 years versus \ge 18 years of age), ppFEV1 determined during the Run-in Period (&lt;70% versus \ge 70\%), SwCI determined during the Run-in Period (&lt;30mmol/L versus \ge 30mmol/L), and prior use of a medication that affects CFTR processing or function (e.g., ELX/TEZ/IVA) use (yes versus no).
L2867: Subjects who completed the 52-day treatment period were able to enroll in the open-label extension study (Trial VX20-121-104), if eligible.
L2868: ## **Trial Population**
L2869: The trials enrolled adults and adolescents (12 years and older) with stable CF per the following enrollment criteria and eligible *CFTR* genotypes as outlined in Table 179.
L2870: ## NDA 218730
