L2880: The additional key eligibility criteria are listed below:
L2881: ## Key Inclusion Criteria
L2882: - Ages 12 years or older
- Confirmed diagnosis of CF as determined by the investigator
- CFTR genotype as reviewed in Table 179
- Percent predicted forced expiratory volume in one second {Quanjer, 2012 #12}:Subjects receiving ELX/TEZ/IVA at screening: ≥40% and ≤90%
- Subjects not receiving ELX/TEZ/IVA at screening: ≥40% and ≤80%
- Stable CF disease as judged by the investigator
L2883: ## **Key Exclusion Criteria**
L2884: - History of comorbidity that might confound the results in the opinion of the investigator. Including, but not limited to:Hepatic cirrhosis with portal hypertension, moderate hepatic impairment (Child Pugh Score 7 to 9), severe hepatic impairment (Child Pugh Score 10 to 15)
- Sold organ or hematological transplantation
- Alcohol or drug abuse in the past year
- Cancer except for squamous cell skin cancer, basal cell skin cancer, and Stage 0 cervical carcinoma in situ
- History of intolerance to trial drug that would pose additional risk to the subject in the opinion of the investigator (e.g., subjects with a history of liver function test (LFT) elevations requiring treatment interruption or discontinuation, allergy or hypersensitivity to the trial drug)
- Any of the following abnormal labs at screening:Hemoglobin &lt;10 g/dL
- Total bilirubin \ge 2 x upper limit of normal (ULN)
- Aspartate transaminase (AST), alanine transaminase (ALT), gamma-glutamyl transferase, or alkaline phosphatase (ALP) \ge 3 x ULN
- Abnormal renal function defined as glomerular filtrate rate \le 50 mL/min/1.73 m^2 (Modification of Diet in Renal Disease Study Equation (Levey et al. 1999; Levey et al. 2006)) for subjects \ge 18 years and \le 45 mL/min/1.73 m^2 (Counahan-Barratt equation (Counahan et al. 1976)) for subjects 12-17 years
- An acute upper or lower respiratory tract infection, infectious pulmonary exacerbation (PEx) of CF, or changes in therapy (including antibiotics) for sinopulmonary disease within 28 days before the first dose of ELX/TEZ/IVA in the run-in period
- Lung infection with organisms associated with a more rapid decline in pulmonary status (including but not limited to *Burkholderia cenocepacia*, *Burkholderia dolosa*, and *Mycobacterium abscessus*). For subjects who have had a history of a positive culture, the investigator will apply the following criteria to establish whether the subject is free of infection with such organisms:The subject has not had a respiratory tract culture positive for these organisms within the 12 months before the date of informed consent
- The subject has at least two respiratory tract cultures negative for these organisms within the 12 months before the day of informed consent, with the first and last of these separated by at least 3 months, and the most recent one within the 6 months before the date of informed consent
L2885: - An acute illness not related CF (e.g., gastroenteritis) within 14 days before the first dose of ELZ/TEZ/IVA in the run-in period
- Ongoing or prior participation in a study of an investigational treatment other than the Applicant's medications that affect CFTR processing or function
- Use of prohibited medications
- Pregnant or breast-feeding females
L2886: The overall population for the phase 3 trials was reasonable and consistent with other similar clinical development programs in the CF population. Note that subjects unable to tolerate ELX/TEZ/IVA were excluded. This is a limitation of the trial design and may affect both the overall generalizability and ability to assess the safety of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA in patients unable to tolerate ELX/TEZ/IVA, an area of unmet need in the CF population, as reviewed in Section 7.7.1.
L2887: ## **Trial Drug Interruption and Stopping Rules**
L2888: ## **Liver Function Tests**
L2889: The protocol stated that the central laboratory would notify the medical monitor of ALT or AST &gt;3 \times ULN and total bilirubin &gt;2 \times ULN. Subjects with new treatment-emergent ALT or AST elevations of &gt;3 \times ULN, with or without total bilirubin &gt;2 \times ULN were followed closely with confirmatory testing within 28 to 72 hours of the initial finding.
L2890: Trial drug administration was interrupted immediately (prior to confirmatory testing) if any of the following criteria were met:
L2891: - ALT or AST &gt;8 \times ULN
- ALT or AST &gt;5 \times ULN for more than 2 weeks
- ALT or AST &gt;3 \times ULN in association with total bilirubin &gt;2 \times ULN and/or clinical jaundice
L2892: A thorough investigation of potential causes was conducted, and the subject was monitored closely for clinical progression.
L2893: Trial drug administration was discontinued if subsequent ALT or AST values confirmed the initial evaluation that satisfied the interruption rule and no convincing alternative etiology (e.g., acetaminophen use, viral hepatitis, alcohol ingestion) was identified, regardless of whether the transaminase levels improved. All subjects for whom treatment was discontinued for elevated transaminases and bilirubin, had levels monitored closely until normalization or return to baseline. If an alternative, reversible cause of transaminase elevation, with or without increased bilirubin or clinical jaundice, was identified, trial drug administration was resumed once the transaminases returned to baseline or were \le 2 \times ULN, whichever was higher. Upon resumption, transaminases and bilirubin were assessed weekly for 4 weeks. Trial drug was permanently discontinued if a protocol-defined transaminase interruption threshold recurred within 4 weeks of the rechallenge of the trial drug.
L2894: ## Rash
L2895: Trial drug dosing was interrupted for subjects who developed a generalized rash Grade 3 or higher or a rash that was considered a serious adverse event (SAE). The investigators were advised to consider additional evaluations and were able to reconsider resumption of trial drug if considered clinically appropriate.
L2896: ## Other
L2897: In addition, subjects were discontinued from trial drug treatment if they met any of the following criteria:
L2898: - Had a screening *CFTR* genotype that does not confirm trial eligibility if a previous *CFTR* genotype laboratory report was used to establish eligibility.
- Became pregnant
- Met any trial drug discontinuation criteria (reviewed above)
L2899: Subjects who withdrew before the first dose of trial drug prior to Day 1 of the Treatment Period were able to be replaced.
L2900: The stopping criteria outlined in the protocol's was acceptable.
L2901: ## Dose and Duration of Treatment
L2902: The dosages evaluated in the phase 3 trials were as follows:
L2903: - Active control: ELX 200 mg QD/TEZ 100 mg QD/IVA 150 mg q12h
- Treatment: VNZ 20 mg QD/TEZ 100 mg QD/D-IVA 250 mg QD
L2904: The trial drug was administered orally with a fat-containing meal or snack. It was recommended that the dose be taken within 30 mins of the start of the meal or snack.
L2905: If six hours or fewer had passed since the missed morning or evening dose, the subject was to take the missed dose as soon as possible and continue on the original schedule. If more than 6 hours had passed since the missed morning dose, the subject was to take the missed dose as soon as possible and not take the evening dose. If more than six hours had passed since the missed evening dose, the subject should not take the missed doses. The next scheduled morning dose was to be taken at the usual time. The morning and evening doses were not to have been taken at the same time.
L2906: All subjects (and their parents/caregivers/companions), site personnel (including investigators, site monitor, and trial team), and members of the Applicant's trial team were blinded to the treatment group. The Applicant's trial team did not have access to the spirometry or SwCl results after the first dose of the trial drug on Day 1 of the treatment period, with the exception of spirometry related adverse event reporting. Individual SwCl results were also not disclosed to the trial sites with the exception of the screening visit and Day -14 values. Subjects and their
L2907: parents/caregivers/companions were not to be informed of trial-related spirometry results until the Applicant determined that the trial had completed.
L2908: Subjects remained on their stable standard-of-care CF regimen with the exception of any alternative medications that affect CFTR processing or function, approved or investigational. The key prohibited and acceptable concomitant medications, reviewed below, were acceptable and consistent with prior phase 3 trials in the CF population.
L2909: ## Key Prohibited Medications
L2910: - Moderate and strong CYP3A inducers (within 14 days before the first dose of ELX/TEZ/IVA through the completion of trial participation)
- Moderate and strong CYP3A inhibitors except ciprofloxacin (within 14 days before the first dose of ELX/TEZ/IVA through the completion of trial participation)
- Non-Vertex medications that affect CFTR processing or function, investigational or approved (none allowed within 28 days or 5 terminal half-lives before screening through completion of trial participation)
- Vertex medications that affect CFTR processing or function, investigational or approved (none allowed from the first dose of ELX/TEZ/IVA on Day -28 until after the last dose of trial drug)
L2911: ## Prior and Concomitant Medications
L2912: - Subjects were to remain on stable treatment regimen for CF from 28 days before the Run-in through completion of trial participation. Guidelines for stable CF treatment regimens included:Subjects on inhaled tobramycin or other chronically inhaled antibiotics should remain on that regimen throughout the trial.
- Subjects on cycle on and off inhaled antibiotics should continue on their prior schedule. The timing of the first dose of ELX/TEZ/IVA in the Run-in Period should be synchronized as closely as possible (e.g., not more than \pm 3 days).
- Subjects who alternate between two different inhaled antibiotics should remain on the same cycling schedule during the trial. The timing of the first dose of ELX/TEZ/IVA in the Run-in Period should be synchronized as closely as possible (e.g., not more than \pm 3 days) to the first day in the cycle onto one of the inhaled antibiotics.
- Subjects may receive doses of prednisone or prednisolone of up to 10 mg/day chronically. Subjects could not receive prednisone or prednisolone at doses of &gt;60 mg daily for longer than a 5-day period.
- Caution and appropriate monitoring should be used when coadministration of trial drugs with medicinal products that are substrates of OATP1B1/1B3, such as statins, glyburide, nateglinide, and repaglinide.
L2913: NDA 218730
L2914: ## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L2915: - Digoxin and other substrates of P-glycoprotein with a narrow therapeutic index, such as cyclosporine, everolimus, sirolimus, and tacrolimus, should be used with caution and appropriate monitoring.
- During coadministration with warfarin, additional monitoring of the international normalized ratio is recommended. Other medicinal products that are CYP2C9 substrates for which exposure may be increased include glimepiride and glipizide; these should be used with caution.
L2916: ## Trial Endpoints
L2917: ## Primary Endpoint
L2918: - Absolute change from baseline in percent predicted forced expiratory volume in 1 second (ppFEV1) through Week 24
L2919: ## Key Secondary Endpoints
L2920: - Absolute change from baseline in SwCl through Week 24
- Proportion of subjects with SwCl &lt;60mmol/L through Week 24 (pooled with data from Trials 102 and 103)
- Proportion of subjects with SwCl &lt;30mmol/L through Week 24 (pooled with data from Trials 102 and 103)
L2921: ## Other Secondary Endpoints
L2922: - Number of PEx through Week 48
- Absolute change from baseline in CFQ-R RD score through Week 24
- Absolute change from baseline in ppFEV1 through Week 48
- Absolute change from baseline in SwCl through Week 48
- Proportion of subjects with SwCl &lt;60mmol/L through Week 24
- Proportion of subjects with SwCl &lt;30mmol/L through Week 24
- Safety and tolerability assessments based on adverse events (AEs), clinical laboratory values, ECGs, vital signs, and pulse oximetry
L2923: ## **Other Endpoints**
L2924: - Proportion of subjects with SwCl &lt;60\text{mmol/L} through Week 48
- Proportion of subjects with SwCl &lt;30\text{mmol/L} through Week 48
- Absolute change from baseline in body mass index (BMI) at Week 48
- Absolute change from baseline in BMI z-score at Week 48
- Absolute change from baseline in weight at Week 48
- Absolute change from baseline in CFQ-R RD score through Week 48
- PK parameters of VNZ, TEZ, and D-IVA
L2925: Overall, the trial endpoints are acceptable and consistent with other similar phase 3 trials in this population. They evaluate a number of factors considered to be clinically meaningful to patients with CF including ppFEV1, CFQ-R RD, BMI, SwCl, weight, and CF PExs, although these endpoints were outside of the statistical hierarchy. Further discussions regarding the use of SwCl-based key secondary endpoints are included in Section 6.3.3.
L2926: ## **Efficacy Assessments**
L2927: ## **Spirometry**
L2928: Spirometry was performed according to the American Thoracic Society/European Respiratory Society guidelines (Graham et al. 2019). Pre-bronchodilator was defined as spirometry performed for subjects who withheld short-acting bronchodilators for \ge 4 hours, long-acting bronchodilator for \ge 12 hours, and once-daily long-acting bronchodilator \ge 24 hours prior to the spirometry assessment.
L2929: During the Screening period, spirometry assessments were able to be performed pre- or post-bronchodilator. At all other visits, spirometry assessments were performed pre-bronchodilator and, during the treatment period, before trial drug dosing at approximately the same time each visit.
L2930: Measured spirometry values were converted to percent predicted values using standard equations of the Global Lung Institute (Quanjer et al. 2012). Note these trials were conducted prior to the recent American Thoracic Society statement on the use race-neutral approaches to pulmonary function test (PFT) interpretation (Bhakta et al. 2023).
L2931: All spirometry data was transmitted to a centralized spirometry service for quality review. The investigator's assessments of spirometry results were used for the screening assessment and determination of eligibility.
L2932: ## **Sweat Chloride**
L2933: SwCl is a pharmacodynamic measure of CFTR function. A SwCl concentration of \ge 60\text{mmol/L} is diagnostic of CF and \le 30\text{mmol/L} is the normal range (Farrell et al. 2017). The trials completed the SwCl test (quantitative pilocarpine iontophoresis) using an approved collection device. The
L2934: protocol required collection before trial drug dosing. At each time point, two samples were collected (one from each arm). Samples were sent to a central laboratory for testing and interpretation.
L2935: ## CF Pulmonary Exacerbation Definition
L2936: A CF pulmonary exacerbation was defined as a new or change in antibiotic therapy (IV, inhaled, or oral) for four or more of the following signs or symptoms:
L2937: - Change in sputum
- New or increased hemoptysis
- Increased cough
- Increased dyspnea
- Malaise, fatigue, or lethargy
- Temperature above 38°C (approximately 100.4°F)
- Anorexia or weight loss
- Sinus pain or tenderness
- Change in sinus discharge
- Change in physical examination of the chest
- Decrease in pulmonary function by 10%
- Radiographic change indicative of pulmonary infection.
L2938: Overall, the definition of CF pulmonary exacerbations used is acceptable and consistent with similar pivotal trials in this population. It was recommended that the trial drug not be interrupted during an exacerbation unless it was determined to be in the best interest of the subject, as determined by the investigator.
L2939: ## Cystic Fibrosis Questionnaire-Revised
L2940: The CFQ-R is a disease-specific health-related quality of life measure for CF that consists of generic and CF-specific scales grouped into three modules and nine domains. The scales measure quality of life, health perception, and symptoms over a 2-week recall period. It is available in age-appropriate versions: child age 6 through 11 years (interview format), child age 12-13 years (self-reported format), adolescent/adult ages \ge 14 years (self-reported), and a parent/caregiver proxy format. The RD of the CFQ-R has also been utilized independently to evaluate for respiratory symptoms relevant to patients with CF. In patients with stable respiratory symptoms and chronic *Pseudomonas aeruginosa* infection, a difference of at least four points in the CFQ-R RD score is generally considered the meaningful within patient change (Quittner et al. 2009).
L2941: Subjects were asked to complete the CFQ-R in their native language if validated translations were available. If no validated translation was available in the subject's native language, the subject did not complete the questionnaire. Subjects 12 and 13 years of age at the date of
L2942: informed consent were asked to complete the CFQ-R Child version themselves, and their parents/caregivers completed the CFQ-R Parent version, at all visits, regardless of whether the subject subsequently turned 14 years of age during the trial. Subjects 14 years of age and older at the date of informed consent completed the Adolescent/Adult version of the questionnaire themselves at all visits.
L2943: ## **Height and Weight**
L2944: Weight and height were measured with shoes off. Following screening, height was collected only for subjects \le 21 years of age on the date of informed consent.
L2945: ## **Ophthalmologic Examination**
L2946: Ophthalmologic examinations were only obtained in subjects &lt;18 years of age on the date of informed consent. All examinations were conducted by a licensed ophthalmologic or optometrist and included the measurement of best-corrected distance visual acuity of each eye and pharmacologically-dilated examination of the lens with a slit lamp. A screening examination was not required if there was documentation of an examination meeting the protocol requirements within the 3 months before the date of informed consent. No examinations were required in subjects with documentation of bilateral lens removal.
L2947: Examinations occurred at screening and within 4 weeks prior to completion of trial participation (in subjects who have completed at least 12 weeks of trial drug treatment).
L2948: ## **Safety**
L2949: Safety evaluations will include AEs, clinical laboratory assessments, clinical evaluation of vital signs, ECGs, PExs, and pulse oximetry.
L2950: ## **Statistical Analysis Plan**
L2951: ## **Sample Size and Power**
L2952: The primary null hypothesis to be tested was that the mean absolute change in ppFEV1 from baseline through Week 24 for VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA is inferior by &gt;3 percentage points compared to ELX/TEZ/IVA.
L2953: The noninferiority (NI) margin represented a clinically acceptable loss of effectiveness as outlined in *Non-Inferiority Clinical Trials to Establish Effectiveness* (November 2016) regulatory guidance. Furthermore, a statistical approach using the Rothmann method recommends that the NI margin preserves at least 50% of the treatment effect of the active control (ELX/TEZ/IVA) compared to placebo (Schumi and Wittes 2011), where the treatment effect is estimated by the lower bound of the 95% CI. In the overall population eligible for Trials 102 and 103, the lower bound of the 95% CI of the treatment effect for ELX/TEZ/IVA was approximately 12 percentage points for ppFEV1. The selected noninferiority margin of three percentage points was consistent with this statistical method for ppFEV1 in clinical studies evaluating CF treatments.
L2954: For Trial 102, assuming a within-group standard deviation (SD) of 8 and a 10% drop-out rate at Week 24 and a treatment difference of 0 between VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA and ELX/TEZ/IVA, a
L2955: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L2956: sample size of 200 subjects in each group for a total of 400 subjects would have more than 90% power to test the primary hypothesis for the primary endpoint, based on a 1-sided, 2-sample t-test at a significance level of 0.025.
L2957: For Trial 103, assuming a within-group standard deviation (SD) of 8 and a 10% drop-out rate at Week 24 and a treatment difference of 0 between VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA and ELX/TEZ/IVA, a sample size of 275 subjects in each group for a total of 550 subjects would have more than 95% power to test the primary hypothesis for the primary endpoint, based on a 1-sided, 2-sample t-test at a significance level of 0.025.
L2958: ## Noninferiority Margin for Trial 103
L2959: Although placebo-controlled data with ELX/TEZ/IVA in direct comparison with placebo was not available for F/F, F/G, and F/RF subjects, a treatment effect could be estimated using Bucher's Indirect Comparison method by pooling data from Studies 445-103/105 and 661-106 for F/F subjects, Studies 445-104 and 770-102 for F/G subjects, and Studies 445-104 and 661-108 for F/RF subjects (see Table 181).

Additionally, the Applicant agreed to clearly define residual function and gating variants in the clinical trial protocol. An analysis of efficacy for the primary endpoint for the variant subgroups (F/F versus non-F/F) was specified in the statistical analysis plan with a proposal to present the estimated treatment effect along the 95% CI (see Figure 4).
L2973: ## **Primary Estimand**
L2974: The primary estimand is defined as the following:
L2975: - Treatment: VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA versus ELX/TEZ/IVA
- Population: Trial population represented by inclusion and exclusion criteria
- Variable: Absolute change from baseline (after ELX/TEZ/IVA run-in) in ppFEV1 through Week 24 (estimated by averaging Weeks 16 and 24)
- Handling of intercurrent events:The treatment policy strategy will be used to handle the use of non-trial drug that affects CFTR processing or function for &gt;3 days in either the Run-in Period or the Treatment Period prior to Week 24, which means that observed ppFEV1 values will be used, if available, after this prohibited medication use.
- The treatment policy strategy will be used to handle treatment discontinuation prior to Week 24, which means that observed ppFEV1 values will be used, if available, after treatment discontinuation.
- Population level summary: Difference in variable means between VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA and ELX/TEZ/IVA groups
L2976: ## **Primary Analysis**
L2977: The primary null hypothesis tested was that the mean for the primary efficacy variable for VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA was inferior by &gt;3 percentage points compared to ELX/TEZ/IVA. The primary analysis of the primary estimand was performed using a mixed-effects model for repeated measures (MMRM) with change from baseline at Day 15, Week 4, Week 8, Week 16, and Week 24 as the dependent variable. For Trial 102, the model included fixed categorical effects for treatment, visit, age at screening (&lt;18 versus \ge18 years of age), and treatment-by-visit interaction, with baseline ppFEV1 and baseline SwCI as continuous covariates. The model estimated using restricted maximum likelihood. Denominator degrees of freedom for the F-test for fixed effects were estimated using the Kenward-Roger approximation. An unstructured covariance structure was used to model the within-subject errors. If the model estimation did not converge, an alternative covariance structure like compound symmetry would have been used. For Trial 103, the model included an additional fixed categorical effect for genotype group (F/F, F/G, F/RF, TCR/non-F). If the model estimation did not converge, genotype group was considered with two levels (F/F and non-F/F) or removed from the model, and if non-convergence persisted, an alternative covariance structure like compound symmetry would have been used.
L2978: Conditional on the observed data and covariates, missing data was assumed to be missing at random.
L2979: The primary result obtained from the model was the estimated treatment difference through Week 24 (estimated by averaging Weeks 16 and 24). The adjusted mean with a 2-sided 95% CI and the 1-sided P value for noninferiority was provided. The primary null hypothesis was rejected, and noninferiority demonstrated, if the lower bound of the CI is \ge3.0.
L2980: The primary null hypothesis of noninferiority described above was tested based on the full analysis set (FAS).
L2981: If noninferiority for the primary endpoint was demonstrated, and if the lower bound of the same 95% CI was greater than 0, then superiority for the primary endpoint was also demonstrated, and the 2-sided P value for superiority would be presented.
L2982: ## Supplementary Analysis
L2983: An alternative estimand was defined similarly to the primary estimand with the exception that intercurrent events were addressed using the hypothetical strategy. In the hypothetical strategy, observed values after intercurrent events were set to missing to provide a conservative and robust test of noninferiority for the primary endpoint. Supplementary analysis of this alternative estimand was performed similarly to the primary analysis.
L2984: ## Subgroup Analysis
L2985: Subgroup analyses of the primary efficacy endpoint were performed for each of the following subgroups:
L2986: - Age at screening (&lt;18; ≥18 years)
- ppFEV1 at baseline (&lt;70; ≥70 percentage points)
- SwCl at baseline (&lt;30; ≥30mmol/L)
- Sex (male; female)
- Geographic region (North America [including United States and Canada]; Rest of the World [including Europe, Israel, Australia, and New Zealand])
L2987: The MMRM used for the primary analysis of the primary estimand was used for the subgroup analyses, where the similar model was applied to each category of the subgroup.
L2988: ## Analysis of Key Secondary Efficacy Variables
L2989: ## Absolute Change From Baseline in SwCl Through Week 24
L2990: The analysis of absolute change from baseline in SwCl through Week 24 was based on an MMRM similar to the primary analysis of the primary estimand, with absolute change from baseline in SwCl at Day 15, Week 4, Week 16, and Week 24 as the dependent variable. The estimated treatment difference through Week 24 (estimated by averaging Weeks 16 and 24) was presented along with the 2-sided 95% CI and P value.
L2991: ## Proportion of Subjects With SwCl &lt;60mmol/L Through Week 24 (Pooled With Data From Trials 102 and 103)
L2992: The response corresponding to SwCl &lt;60mmol/L at each visit through Week 24 in the pooled FAS was analyzed using a generalized estimating equations model. The model included fixed categorical effects for treatment, age at screening (&lt;18 versus ≥18 years of age), genotype group (F/MF, F/F, F/G, F/RF, TCR/non-F), visit, and treatment-by-visit interaction, with baseline
L2993: ppFEV1 and baseline SwCl as continuous covariates. A logit link function and an unstructured working correlation matrix were used in the generalized estimating equations model. If the model estimation did not converge, genotype group was considered with three levels (F/MF, F/F, and Other) or removed from the model, and if nonconvergence persists, an alternative covariance structure like compound symmetry would have been used. The estimated odds ratio through Week 24 (estimated by averaging Weeks 16 and 24) along with the 2-sided 95% CI and P value was presented. The estimates for each visit through Week 24 were also presented.
L2994: ## Proportion of Subjects With SwCl &lt;30mmol/L Through Week 24 (Pooled With Data From Trials 102 and 103)
L2995: The analysis was similar to that for the pooled analysis of proportion of subjects with SwCl &lt;60mmol/L through Week 24.
L2996: ## **Multiplicity Adjustment**
L2997: A hierarchical testing procedure was used to control the overall type I error at an alpha of 0.05. The key secondary endpoints were formally tested at an alpha level of 0.05 only if the primary analysis of absolute change from baseline in ppFEV1 through Week 24 was statistically significant, i.e., if the null hypothesis of inferiority had been rejected. For a test at any step to be considered statistically significant within the testing hierarchy, it must be statistically significant, and all previous tests (if any) within the hierarchy must be statistically significant at the 0.05 level (one-sided 0.025 level for the primary endpoint). The testing order of the key secondary endpoints was as follows:
L2998: - Absolute change from baseline in SwCl through Week 24
- Proportion of subjects with SwCl &lt;60mmol/L through Week 24 (pooled with data from Trials 102 and 103)
- Proportion of subjects with SwCl &lt;30mmol/L through Week 24 (pooled with data from Trials 102 and 103)
L2999: ## **Analysis of Other Secondary Efficacy Variables**
L3000: ## Pulmonary Exacerbations
L3001: The number of PEx through Week 52 was analyzed using a negative binomial model with term for treatment, and the estimated PEx rate (per year, with 48 weeks as 1 year) was presented. The difference in PEx rate and the associated 95% CI was provided.
L3002: ## Absolute Change From Baseline in CFQ-R RD Score Through Week 24
L3003: The analysis of absolute change from baseline in CFQ-R RD score through Week 24 was based on an MMRM similar to the primary analysis of the primary estimand, with absolute change from baseline in CFQ-R RD score at Week 8, Week 16, and Week 24 as the dependent variable. The estimated treatment difference through Week 24 (estimated by averaging Weeks 16 and 24) was presented along with the 2-sided 95% CI.
L3004: ## Absolute Change From Baseline in ppFEV1 Through Week 52
L3005: Analysis of this endpoint was based on an MMRM similar to the primary analysis of the primary estimand, with the addition of data from Week 36 and Week 52. The estimated change through Week 52 was based on averaging estimates from Weeks 16, 24, 36 and 52.
L3006: ## Absolute change from baseline in SwCl through Week 52
L3007: Analysis of this endpoint was based on an MMRM similar to that for absolute change from baseline in SwCl through Week 24, with the addition of data from Week 36 and Week 52. The estimated change through Week 52 was based on averaging estimates from Weeks 16, 24, 36 and 52.
L3008: ## **Analysis Population Definitions**
L3009: - All subject set: all subjects who were randomized or received at least one dose of trial drug
- FAS: all randomized subjects who carry the intended *CFTR* allele variant(s) and received at least one dose of trial drug during the treatment period
- Safety analysis set: Included all subjects who received at least one dose of trial drug. This was broken down into the Safety Set for the Run-in Period (subjects who received at least one dose of trial drug during the Run-in Period) and the Safety Set for the Treatment Period (subjects who received at least one dose of trial drug during the Treatment Period).
- Per-protocol set: a subset of the FAS that excluded subjects with certain important protocol deviations and other situations
L3010: ## **Adverse Events**
L3011: The Applicant provided accurate definitions of AEs and SAEs consistent with 21 CFR 312.32. Trial assessments including laboratory tests, ECGs, PExs, and vital signs were assessed and those deemed to have clinically significant worsening from baseline were documented as an AE. All AEs were collected from the time of informed consent until the subject completed the trial participation. Treatment-emergent (TE) time periods were broken down into TE period of the run-in period (from the first dose date of trial drug in the run-in period to the first dose date of the trial drug in the treatment period) and the TE period for the treatment period (from the first dose of trial drug in the treatment period to 28 days after the last dose of the trial drug or to the complete of trial participation, whichever occurs first).
L3012: AE severity was graded using the Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE), Version 5.0, Cancer Therapy Evaluation Program toxicity scale or as mild, moderate, severe, life-threatening, or death. Causality was assessed as related, possibly related, unlikely related, and not related. AEs were coded using the MedDRA dictionary version 26.1.
L3013: Overall, the Applicant's AE and SAE definitions and use of CTCAE severity scale were acceptable and consistent with other CF development programs.
L3014: ## **Adverse Events of Special Interest**
L3015: Liver function test abnormalities, creatinine kinase elevations, rash, cataracts, hypoglycemic events, and neuropsychiatric events were designated as adverse events of special interest (AESIs). These were considered appropriate given the known safety profile of medications similar to VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA (e.g., ELX/TEZ/IVA) and a review of the safety information from the phase 2 development program.
L3016: ## **Protocol Amendments**
L3017: Trial 102, initially dated April 27, 2021, was amended two times. The key changes in each amendment are noted below:
L3018: - Version 2.0 (July 12, 2021) – Clarified eligible MF definition.
- Version 3.0 (August 19, 2021) – Increased sample size and treatment duration (with updates to trial endpoints to reflect this), added designation of AESIs (reviewed above), added that the analysis of the primary and key secondary endpoints through Week 24 will be estimated by averaging Week 16 and 14, and provided rationale for the noninferiority margin for the primary analysis.
L3019: Trial 103, also initially dated April 27, 2021, was amended two times. The key changes in each amendment are noted below:
L3020: - Version 2.0 (July 12, 2021) – Minor changes.
- Version 3.0 (August 19, 2021) – Expanded trial population to include subjects who have at least one *CFTR* variant identified as ELX/TEZ/IVA responsive based on in vitro data and no *F508del* variant, increased sample size and treatment duration (with updates to trial endpoints to reflect this), added designation of AESIs (reviewed above), included that stratification by genotype was to be based on F/F, F/G, F/RF, and TCR/non-F genotypes, added that the analysis of the primary and key secondary endpoints through Week 24 will be estimated by averaging Week 16 and 14, and provided rationale for the noninferiority margin for the primary analysis.
L3021: ## **COVID-19 Impact**
L3022: The trial protocols include safety measures to provide subjects the opportunity to continue to participate in the trial while ensuring safety and minimizing risk of coronavirus disease of 2019 (COVID-19). The Applicant reports that in general, there the impact of COVID-19 on the trial conduct was minimal.
L3023: ## **Protocol Deviations**
L3024: An important protocol deviation was defined as any protocol deviation that may have significant impact the completeness, accuracy, and/or reliability of key trial data or that may have significantly impacted a subject's rights, safety, or well-being.
L3025: In Trial 102, the Applicant reported that 20 (5.0%) of subjects had 21 important protocol deviations (VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA: 11, 5.6%; ELX/TEZ/IVA: 9, 4.5%). The deviation summaries
L3026: provided were reviewed and included subject use of prohibited concomitant medications (e.g., commercially available medications that affect CFTR processing or function while an SAE was investigated) and enrollment of subjects who did not meet eligibility criteria (e.g., use of antibiotics or acute respiratory infection during the run-in period).
L3027: In Trial 103, the Applicant reported that 27 (4.7%) of subjects had important protocol deviations (VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA: 15, 5.3%; ELX/TEZ/IVA: 12, 4.2%). The deviation summaries provided were reviewed and included enrollment of subjects who did not meet eligibility criteria (e.g., use of antibiotics or acute respiratory infection during the run-in period) and use of prohibited concomitant medications (e.g., commercially available or compassionate-use medications that affect CFTR drug processing or function) when protocol-defined criteria were met (e.g., AST levels &gt;8\times ULN). The number of subjects with protocol deviations related specifically to the investigational product (e.g., trial drug dispensation errors, compliance, trial drug kits not being returned, and incorrect trial drug dosing) occurred at higher rates in the VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA treatment arm (11, 3.9%) than the ELX/TEZ/IVA treatment arm (5, 1.7%).
L3028: Overall, the rates protocol deviations were small and consistent with other similarly sized trials. The reported protocol deviations are unlikely to affect the overall analysis of the phase 3 pivotal trials.
L3029: ## 15.2. Pediatric Cohort (6 Through 11 Years Old),
## Trial 105
L3030: ## Overview and Objectives
L3031: Trial VX21-121-105 (Trial 105) was a phase 3, 2-part (Parts A and B), open-label, multicohort, multicenter trial evaluating the PK, safety, tolerability, and efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA triple combination therapy in subjects with CF 1 through 11 years of age. The Applicant submitted the interim analysis report of the final data from subjects 6 through 11 years of age (Cohorts A1 and B1) concurrently with the pivotal phase 3 trials in adults in adolescents.
L3032: **Trial 105 Title:** A Phase 3 Study Evaluating the Pharmacokinetics, Safety, and Tolerability of VX-121 [VNZ] /Tezacaftor/Deutivacaftor Triple Combination Therapy [VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA] in Cystic Fibrosis Subjects 1 Through 11 Years of Age.
L3033: - Cohort 1 Trial dates: June 21, 2022 to November 27, 2023
- Cohort 1 Trial report date: April 3, 2024
L3034: The design of Cohort 1 and the overall pediatric multicohort trial is consistent with other trials performed to support pediatric indications in this population. The consideration of efficacy extrapolation for the 6- to 11-year-old population from the adult and adolescent population is appropriate given the similarly in the disease process between the populations.
L3035: ## Part A Primary Objectives
L3036: - To evaluate the PK of VNZ, TEZ, D-IVA and relative metabolites when dosed in triple combination
- To evaluate the safety and tolerability of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA
L3037: ## Part B Primary Objective
L3038: - To evaluate the safety and tolerability of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA through Week 24
L3039: ## Part B Secondary Objectives
L3040: - To evaluate the efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA through Week 24
- To evaluate the PK or VNZ, TEZ, D-IVA, and relevant metabolites
L3041: ## Trial Design
L3042: Trial 105 was a phase 3, 2-part (Parts A and B), multicenter, multicohort, open-label trial in CF subjects 1 through 11 years of age (inclusive). The cohorts included in Trial 105 were based on subject age:
L3043: - Cohorts A1 and B1: Subjects 6 through 11 years of age
- Cohorts A2 and B2: Subjects 2 through 5 years of age
- Cohorts A3 and B3: Subjects 1 to less than 2 years of age
L3044: This review will include and refer to Cohorts A1 and B1 only unless otherwise noted. The remainder of the cohorts in Trial 105 are ongoing or have not yet started enrollment at the time of the review.
L3045: In Part A, the PK portion, there was a 28-day screening period followed by a 15-day treatment period. PK samples were collected on Days 1, 8, and 15 of the treatment period. A final safety follow-up visit occurred 28 days after the last dose of the trial drug. Trial subjects were eligible to be screened for enrollment in Part B. A review of safety, tolerability, and available PK data was completed by the Applicant's internal team after Cohort A1 completed treatment to determine the doses and weight cutoffs for Cohort B1.
L3046: Part B, the primary safety portion, also included a 28-day screening period. Subjects who were not already receiving stable ELX/TEZ/IVA treatment entered the 4-week (28-day) run-in period and received the approved ELX/TEZ/IVA dose. Stable ELX/TEZ/IVA treatment was defined as receiving ELX/TEZ/IVA for at least 28 days prior to the screening visit. This run-in period was waived for subjects already receiving stable ELX/TEZ/IVA. During the treatment period, all subjects received VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA for 24 weeks. Eligible subjects were able to enroll in the optional open-label extension trial (Trial VX22-121-106). A safety follow-up visit occurred at 28 (\pm 7) days after the last dose of the trial drug but was not required for those who completed the treatment period and transitioned within 28 days of the last dose of trial drug to one of the following: a commercially available Applicant medication that affects CFTR processing or
L3047: function regimen, a managed access program-supplied Applicant medication that affects CFTR processing or function regimen, or an open-label study or other qualified Vertex study. The trial schematic for Part B is shown below in Figure 100.
L3048: **Figure 100. Trial Schematic, Trial 105 (Part B)**
L3049: Source: Applicant Provided (Protocol VX21-121-105, Version 4.0. Figure 9-1)
L3050: Abbreviations: D-IVA, deutivacaftor; ELX, elxacaftor; IVA, ivacaftor; TEZ, tezacaftor; VX-121 [VNZ], vanzacaftor
L3051: Subjects were eligible to be rescreened once. All screening assessments were repeated with the exception of *CFTR* genotyping and ophthalmological examination (if performed within 3 months of the day of informed consent).
L3052: ## Trial Population
L3053: Trial 105 enrolled pediatric subjects (ages 6 through 11 years) with stable CF per the enrollment criteria outlined below:
L3054: ## **Key Inclusion Criteria**
L3055: - Ages 6 through 11 years (inclusive), on the date of informed consentSubjects who completed Cohort A1 but were \ge 12 years of age on the date of informed consent in Cohort B1 are not eligible to enroll in Cohort B1.
- Weight between 5th and 9th percentile (inclusive) for weight-for-age at the Screening Visit based on current CDC growth charts
- Confirmed diagnosis of CF as determined by the investigator
- At least one triple combination responsive variant (including *F508del*) in the *CFTR* gene
- FEV_1 \ge 60\% predicted at screening (Quanjer et al. 2012)
- Stable CF disease as judged by the investigator
- Negative serum pregnancy test at screening for female subjects of childbearing potential
L3056: ## Key Exclusion Criteria
L3057: - History of comorbidity that might confound the results in the opinion of the investigator. Including, but not limited to:Hepatic cirrhosis with portal hypertension, moderate hepatic impairment (Child Pugh Score 7 to 9), severe hepatic impairment (Child Pugh Score 10 to 15)
- Chronic kidney disease (Stage 3 or above)
- Sold organ or hematological transplantation
- Alcohol or drug abuse in the past year
- Cancer
- Any clinically significant laboratory abnormalities at screening that would interfere with the trial assessments or pose an under risk for the subject
- History of intolerance to trial drug that would pose additional risk to the subject in the opinion of the investigator (e.g., subjects with a history of LFT elevations requiring treatment interruption or discontinuation, allergy, or hypersensitivity to the trial drug)
- Any of the following abnormal labs at screening:Hemoglobin &lt;10 g/dL
- Total bilirubin \ge 2 x ULN
- AST, ALT, gamma-glutamyl transferase, or alkaline phosphatase (ALP) \ge 3 x ULN
- Abnormal renal function defined as glomerular filtration rate \le 45 mL/min/1.73 m^2 (Counahan et al. 1976)
- An acute upper or lower respiratory tract infection, infectious PEx of CF, or changes in therapy (including antibiotics) for sinopulmonary disease within 28 days before Day 1 (first dose of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA)
- Lung infection with organisms associated with a more rapid decline in pulmonary status (including but not limited to *Burkholderia cenocepacia*, *Burkholderia dolosa*, and *Mycobacterium abscessus*). For subjects who have had a history of a positive culture, the investigator will apply the following criteria to establish whether the subject is free of infection with such organisms:The subject has not had a respiratory tract culture positive for these organisms within the 12 months before the date of informed consent/assent
- The subject has at least two respiratory tract cultures negative for these organisms within the 12 months before the day of informed consent, with the first and last of these separated by at least 3 months, and the most recent one within the 6 months before the date of informed consent/assent
L3058: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L3059: - An acute illness not related CF (e.g., gastroenteritis) within 14 days before Day 1 (the first dose of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA)
- Ongoing or prior participation in a trial of an investigational treatment other than Vertex medications that affect CFTR processing or function
L3060: The inclusion and exclusion criteria were reasonable to define the trial population and support the proposed indication. The inclusion and exclusion criteria are similar to that used in the pivotal phase 3 trials reviewed in Section 15.1 and for other CF development programs in this age-range.
L3061: ## **Trial Drug Interruption and Stopping Rules**
L3062: The protocol for Trial 105 included trial drug interruption and stopping rules related to liver function testing and rash as in the Trials 102 and 103 (outlined in more detail in Section 15.1).
L3063: ## **Dose and Duration of Treatment**
L3064: A review of safety, tolerability, and available PK data was completed by the Applicant's internal team after Cohort A1 completed treatment to determine the doses and weight cutoffs for Cohort B1. Further discussion of the PK and dosing regimen for Trial 105 appears in Section 14.2.6. The doses and weight cutoffs for Trial 105 are summarized below.

L3067: Source: Clinical Reviewer based on Applicant Provided data
L3068: Abbreviations: D-IVA, deutivacaftor; ELX, elexacaftor; IVA, ivacaftor; q12h, dose every 12 hours; QD, once daily; TEZ, tezacaftor; VNZ, vanzacaftor
L3069: The trial drug was administered orally with a fat-containing meal or snack. It was recommended that the dose be taken within 30 mins of the start of the meal or snack. Additional instructions were provided for missed doses were consistent with those outlined in Trials 102 and 103 (Section 15.1).
L3070: All subjects (and their parents/caregivers/companions), site personnel (including investigators, site monitor, and trial team), and members of the Applicant's trial team were blinded to the treatment group. The Applicant's trial team did not have access to the spirometry or SwCl results after the first dose of the trial drug on Day 1 of the treatment period, with the exception of spirometry related adverse event reporting. Individual SwCl results were also not disclosed to the trial sites with the exception of the screening visit and Day-14 values. Subjects and their
L3071: parents/caregivers/companions were not to be informed of trial-related spirometry results until the Applicant determined that the trial had completed.
L3072: Subjects remained on their stable standard-of-care CF regimen with the exception of any alternative medications that affect CFTR processing or function, approved or investigational. Subjects were permitted to remain on approved medications that affect CFTR processing or function (e.g., IVA, TEZ/IVA, or ELZ/TEZ/IVA) during the screening period with the exception of lumacaftor/IVA that was not allowed within the 14 days prior to the first dose of trial drug in Part A or the first dose of trial drug in the run-in period of Part B. The key prohibited and acceptable concomitant medications were acceptable and consistent with prior phase 3 trials in the CF population. Refer to Section 15.1 for a complete list of medications.
L3073: ## Trial Endpoints
L3074: ## **Part A (Cohort 1)**
L3075: ## Primary Endpoints
L3076: - PK parameters of VNZ, TEZ, D-IVA, and relevant metabolites
- Safety and tolerability as determined by AEs, clinical laboratory values, standard 12-lead ECGs, vital signs, and pulse oximetry
L3077: ## **Part B (Cohort 1)**
L3078: ## Primary Endpoint
L3079: - Safety and tolerability as determined by AEs, clinical laboratory values, standard 12-lead ECGs, vital signs, and pulse oximetry
L3080: ## Secondary Endpoints
L3081: - Absolute change in SwCl from baseline through Week 24
- PK parameters of VNZ, TEZ, D-IVA, and relevant metabolites
- Drug acceptability assessment using Modified Facial Hedonic Scale
- Absolute change in ppFEV1 from baseline through Week 24
- Number of PEx and CF-related hospitalizations through Week 24
- Absolute change in CFQ-R RD score from baseline through Week 24
- Absolute change in BMI and BMI-for-age z-score from baseline at Week 24
- Absolute change in weight and weight-for-age z-score from baseline at Week 24
- Absolute change in height and height-for-age z-score from baseline at Week 24
- Proportion of subjects with SwCl &lt;60mmol/L through Week 24
- Proportion of subjects with SwCl &lt;30mmol/L through Week 24
L3082: ## Other Endpoints
L3083: - Absolute change in FE-1 levels from baseline at Week 24
- Absolute change in serum levels of immunoreactive trypsinogen from baseline at Week 24
- Absolute change in fecal calprotectin levels from baseline at Week 24
- Absolute change in lung clearance index 2.5 from baseline through Week 24
- Absolute change in fat soluble vitamins from baseline through Week 24
- Number of PEx and CF-related hospitalizations through Week 24
L3084: Overall, the endpoints are acceptable and consistent with similar CF trials in this population. In addition, the secondary efficacy endpoints in Part B evaluate a number of factors considered to be clinically meaningful to patients with CF including ppFEV1, CFQ-R, BMI, SwCl, weight, and CF PExs.
L3085: ## Efficacy Assessments
L3086: Efficacy assessments, including pulmonary function testing, SwCl measurements, CFQ-R, definition of CF PEx, and ophthalmological examinations were the same as Trials 102 and 103 and outlined in more detailed in Section 15. Height and weight were measured with shoes off prior to trial drug administration on the days of the in-person clinical visits.
L3087: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L3088: The statistical analyses were descriptive. The analysis plan was outlined in Section 6.2.4.3.
L3089: ## Adverse Events
L3090: The Applicant provided accurate definitions of AEs and SAEs consistent with 21 CFR 312.32. trial assessments including laboratory tests, ECGs, PExs, and vital signs were assessed and those deemed to have clinically significant worsening from baseline were documented as an AE. All AEs were collected from the time of informed consent until the subject completed the trial participation. TE time periods were broken down in to TE period of the run-in period (Part B only, from the first dose date of trial drug in the run-in period to the first dose date of the trial drug in the treatment period) and the TE period for the treatment period (from the first dose of trial drug in the treatment period to 28 days after the last dose of the trial drug or to the complete of trial participation, whichever occurs first).
L3091: AE severity was graded using the CTCAE, Version 5.0, Cancer Therapy Evaluation Program toxicity scale or as mild, moderate, severe, life-threatening, or death. Causality was assessed as related, possibly related, unlikely related, and not related. AEs were coded using the MedDRA dictionary version 26.1.
L3092: Overall, the Applicant's AE and SAE definitions and use of CTCAE severity scale were acceptable and consistent with Trials 102 and 103 and with other CF development programs in this population.
L3093: NDA 218730
L3094: ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L3095: AESIs were the same as the phase 3 trials in adolescents and adults and are outlined in more detail in Section 15.1.
L3096: ## **Protocol Amendments**
L3097: Trial 105, initially dated December 1, 2021, was amended three times. The key changes in each amendment are noted below:
L3098: - Version 2.0 (May 12, 2022) – Added VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA doses and weight cut-offs for Cohort B1 based on preliminary population-PK modeling.
- Version 3.0 (July 15, 2022) – Added specifications regarding postdose PK sampling at Week 8 for subjects \ge 17 kg and removed vitamin and blood biomarker collection in Cohort B1 for &lt;20 kg. These changes were to reduce the total blood drawn from the subjects, if appropriate.
- Version 4.0 (December 19, 2022) – Updated doses administered and weight cutoffs for Cohort B1 based on emerging PK and safety data from Cohort A1 and other phase 3 studies evaluating VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA.
L3099: ## **COVID-19 Impact**
L3100: The trial protocol included safety measures to provide subjects the opportunity to continue participate in the trial while ensuring safety and minimizing risk of COVID-19. The Applicant reports that in general, the impact of COVID-19 on the trial conduct was minimal.
L3101: ## **Protocol Deviations**
L3102: An important protocol deviation was defined as any protocol deviation that may have significantly impacted the completeness, accuracy, and/or reliability of key trial data or that may have significantly impacted a subject's rights, safety, or well-being.
L3103: In Part A, two subjects were reported as having protocol deviations due to enrollment without documented review of screening laboratory analyses and no baseline hemoglobin value confirmed prior to screening. In Part B, an additional two subjects were reported as having important protocol deviations both related to incorrect dispensation of trial drug (both subjects received the incorrect (\ge 40 kg) kit at Day 1 but where dispensed the correct (&lt;40 kg) kit on Day 15). The rates of protocol deviations were small and unlikely to affect the overall analysis of Trial 105.

L3175: ## ppFEV1 Through Week 52
L3176: The Applicant evaluated the absolute change from baseline of the ppFEV1 through Week 52, which the Applicant defined as the average of the estimated treatment effects in Weeks 16, 24,
L3177: 36, and 52. These endpoints supported the primary endpoint. As shown in Table 194, the mean (95% CI) change from baseline in ppFEV1 through 52 weeks was 0.1% (-0.8%, 1.0%) in Trial 102 and 0.3 (-0.4%, 1.0%) in Trial 103.
L3178: ## SwCl Through Week 52
L3179: The Applicant also evaluated the absolute change from baseline of the SwCl through Week 52, which the Applicant defined as the average of the estimated treatment effects in Weeks 16, 24, 36, and 52. VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA treatment led to lower SwCl through 52 weeks compared to ELX/TEZ/IVA. The mean (95% CI) absolute change in SwCl through 52 weeks was -8.0 (-9.9, -6.1) mmol/L and -2.8 (-4.6, -1.0) mmol/L in Trials 102 and 103, respectively. The clinical significance of this unclear and reviewed in more detail in Section 6.3.2.
L3180: ## Responder Analysis: Proportion of Subjects With SwCl &lt;60mmol/L Through Week 24
L3181: The Applicant presented the odds ratio for the proportion of subjects with a SwCl &lt;60mmol/L for the pooled phase 3 population and by individual trial. Subjects treated with VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA were more likely to have a SwCl &lt;60mmol/L through Week 24 than subjects treated with ELX/TEZ/IVA in the pooled phase 3 population: odds ratio (95% CI): 2.21 (1.55, 3.15), p&lt;0.0001. Similar results were demonstrated when the phase 3 trials were analyzed separately, although in Trial 103, the 95% confidence interval did not exclude the null. These results are supportive of the efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA, but the overall clinical significance is unclear, as reviewed in more detail in Section 6.3.2.
L3182: ## Responder Analysis: Proportion of Subjects With SwCl &lt;30mmol/L Through Week 24
L3183: The Applicant also presented the odds ratio for the proportion of subjects with a SwCl &lt;30mmol/L. Similar to the responder analysis of subjects below the CF diagnostic threshold, subjects treated with VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA were more likely to have a SwCl &lt;30mmol/L through Week 24 than subjects treated with ELX/TEZ/IVA in the pooled phase 3 population: odds ratio (95% CI): 2.87 (2.00, 4.12), p&lt;0.0001. Similar results were demonstrated when the phase 3 trials were analyzed separately, and for this analysis, the 95% confidence intervals excluded the null in both trials. These results are supportive of the efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA, but the overall clinical significance is unclear, as reviewed in more detail in Section 6.3.2.
L3184: ## 16.2.2. Pediatric Cohort (6 Through 11 Years Old),
## Trial 105
L3185: ## Secondary Efficacy Endpoints
L3186: As noted previously, efficacy in the 6- to 11-year-old CF population was extrapolated from adults and adolescents. The Applicant also evaluated a number of exploratory efficacy endpoints in the 6- to 11-year-old population as reviewed in Table 195. The lack of a control arm limits the ability to interpret these data, but, in general, the results support the efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA in this age range.

L3193: ## Drug Acceptability Assessment at Week 24
L3194: The drug acceptability assessment was evaluated using the Modified Hedonic Scale at Week 24. Only 2 out of 74 subjects (2.8%) indicated that they disliked the medication (“disliked it a little” or “disliked it very much”) and 24 (32.4%) subjects responded that they were neutral. The remainder of subjects 48 (64.8%) indicated they liked the medication (“liked it a little” or “disliked it very much”). These results are supportive of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA in this population.
L3195: ## Number of PEx and CF-Related Hospitalizations Through Week 24
L3196: The Applicant recorded the number of PEx including PEx requiring hospitalization or IV antibiotics therapy. Overall, six subjects (7.7%) experienced PExs with one subject (1.3%) hospitalized and requiring IV antibiotic therapy. The lack of comparator arm limits the analysis of these data, but these rates appear to be consistent with published data in CF patients in this age range.
L3197: ## CFQ-R RD Through Week 24
L3198: Treatment with VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA lead to an increase in the mean CFQ-R RD score through 24 weeks of 3.9 points. This is supportive of the efficacy of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA. The magnitude of the absolute mean change from baseline is smaller than the changes observed in the ELX/TEZ/IVA trial in this population but higher than the adult and adolescent population in the phase 3 trials. The significance of this difference is unclear given the lack of control arm.
L3199: ## BMI, Weight, Height, and BMI Z-Scores at Week 24
L3200: Treatment with VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA resulted in within subject increases in absolute change from baseline for weight (1.67 kg), height (2.7 cm), and BMI (0.22 kg/m2) at Week 24. BMI z-scores remained similar over this period (-0.05). The assessment of these changes is limited by the absence of a comparator arm, and it is unclear if the observed absolute increases in weight and height reflect normal development or a treatment effect. Based on CDC growth charts for healthy
L3201: ## NDA 218730
## ALYFTREK (vanzacaftor/tezacaftor/deutivacaftor) Tablets
L3202: children, the average weight gain and growth for children between the age of 6 through 11 years is approximately 1-3 kg and 4-6 cm (NCHS 2024). While the observed change in stature is below the CDC growth chart averages in healthy children, the stability of the BMI z-score is reassuring.
L3203: ## Proportion of Subjects With SwCl &lt;60mmol/L or &lt;30mmol/L Through Week 24
L3204: The mean SwCl at Week 24 was 40.4mmol/L. The proportion of subjects with a SwCl less than 60mmol/L was 94.9% at Week 24 and 52.6% of subjects had a SwCl less than 30mmol/L at Week 24. The ability to interpret these results is limited by the lack of control arm. In addition, the clinical meaningfulness of these endpoints is unclear, as discussed in further detail in Section 6.3.2.
L3205: ## Other Efficacy Endpoints
L3206: The Applicant also evaluated multiple other efficacy endpoints including fecal elastase-1, serum immunoreactive trypsinogen, fecal calprotectin, lung clearance index 2.5, and levels of the fat-soluble vitamins. The evaluation of these results is limited because of the lack of comparator arm and because these are exploratory endpoints.

L3247: Abbreviations: AE, adverse event; ELX, elaxacaftor; IVA, ivacaftor; N, number of subjects in treatment group; n, number of subjects in subset; SAE, serious adverse event; SAS, safety analysis set; TEZ, tezacaftor
L3248: Overall, 395 (38.3%) of subjects reported an AE during the run-in period with 11 (1.1%) subjects reporting an SAE. The most common AEs reported were headache, cough, and COVID-19. The most common AE leading to drug discontinuation was infective pulmonary exacerbation of CF.
L3249: Overall, the review of the AEs in the run-in period did not reveal any new safety signals.
L3250: ## Adverse Events in the Follow-Up Period
L3251: The Applicant reported that 822 (84.6%) subjects from Trials 102 and 103 enrolled in the open-label extension, Trial VX20-121-104 (Trial 104). Of those subjects who did not enroll in Trial 104 and entered the run-in period, the review of AEs and SAEs did not reveal any new safety concerns. The most common AE reported during this time was infective pulmonary exacerbation of CF.
L3252: ## 17.3. Adverse Events, Trial 105 (Cohort A1)
L3253: AEs occurring in Cohort A1 of Trial 105 were also reviewed. Overall, 12 (70.6%) subjects experienced an AE. All AEs were considered mild to moderate and 3 (17.6%) subjects experienced AEs that were determined to be related by the investigator. There were no deaths and no SAEs reported during Cohort A1. The most common AEs reported in subjects were cough (3, 17.6%), abdominal pain (2, 11.8%), and rash (2, 11.8%). There were no new safety signals identified during the review of the safety data from Cohort A1 of Trial 105.
L3254: ## 17.4. Additional Safety Analyses
L3255: ## Electrocardiograms
L3256: No clinically significant ECG trends or safety concerns were identified in the phase 3 trials of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA. We note there was a numerically higher proportion of subjects in the VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA treatment group with a decrease of \ge 20 beats per minute from baseline compared to the ELX/TEZ/IVA (8.6% versus 6.5%). This finding is unlikely to be clinically significant. AEs related to ECG findings were similar between the treatment groups.
L3257: ## QT Study
L3258: VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA did not prolong the QTcF interval in the thorough QT study assessment (Study VX22-121-013). This was reviewed by the Interdisciplinary Review team for Cardiac Safety Studies (review dated July 16, 2024).
L3259: ## Safety Specific Clinical Trials
L3260: The Applicant submitted a 90-day safety update that consisted of updates that became available since the safety data cut for the NDA submission and included any deaths, all new SAEs, AEs leading to treatment discontinuation, and pregnancies from February 13, 2024, to May 15, 2024, for ongoing studies in the VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA program.

L3282: ## 23. Labeling: Key Changes
L3283: This Prescribing Information (PI) review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for major changes to the finalized PI as compared to the Applicant's draft PI (Table 202). The PI was reviewed to ensure that PI meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent with labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate information needed for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise information for the healthcare practitioner.
L3284: **Table 202. Key Labeling Changes and Considerations**
L3285: Full PI Sections1	Rationale for Major Changes to Finalized PI2 Compared to Applicant's Draft PI
BOXED WARNING	A Boxed Warning for the risk of drug-induced liver injury and liver failure was added.
1 INDICATIONS AND USAGE	The established pharmacological class information was updated.
2 DOSAGE AND ADMINISTRATION	(b) (4) was removed.
4 CONTRAINDICATIONS	
5 WARNINGS AND PRECAUTIONS	The Warnings and Precautions were updated to include information on the risk for drug-induced liver injury and liver failure with recommendations for monitoring of LFTs.
A warning was added regarding the potential risks and uncertainty in patients who discontinued or interrupted ELX, TEZ, or IVA containing drugs due to adverse reactions (ARs).
A warning was added to include the risk of hypersensitivity reactions, consistent with ELX/TEZ/IVA.
The remaining warnings and precautions include the risk of reduced effectiveness with concomitant use of CYP3A inducers, the risk of ARs with concomitant use with CYP3A inhibitors, and the risk of cataracts. The Warnings and Precautions are similar to those of ELX/TEZ/IVA and medications that contain one or more of its components.

6 ADVERSE REACTIONS	This section was updated to include potential reactions based on the data from the phase 3 clinical trials. As these were noninferiority trials without a placebo-controlled treatment arm, ARs were defined as events occurring at or above a threshold of 5% and ≥1% higher frequency in the VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA exposed patients compared to ELX/TEZ/IVA exposed patients.
A caveat was added to inform prescribers that there is no data available in patients who could not tolerate ELX/TEZ/IVA.
Additional data on LFTs was added, including a dedicated table.

7 DRUG INTERACTIONS	(b) (4) were removed.
L3286: Full PI Sections1	Rationale for Major Changes to Finalized PI2 Compared to Applicant's Draft PI
8 USE IN SPECIFIC POPULATIONS (e.g., Pregnancy, Lactation, Females and Males of Reproductive Potential, Pediatric Use, Geriatric Use, Renal Impairment, Hepatic Impairment)	Nonclinical information was updated to be in proper PLLR format, per the FDA draft guidance for industry Pregnancy, Lactation, and Reproductive Potential: Labeling for Human Prescription Drug and Biological Products—Content and Format (July 2020).
9 DRUG ABUSE AND DEPENDENCE	Not applicable.
10 OVERDOSAGE	
12 CLINICAL PHARMACOLOGY	Drug-drug interaction content was reorganized by in vitro/in vivo source (b) (4).
The table of indicated mutations was reorganized by data source (e.g., clinical trials, in vitro data).

13 NONCLINICAL TOXICOLOGY	Juvenile toxicology information was removed from this section and added to Section 8.4 to adhere to the PLLR guidance format guidelines (July 2020).
14 CLINICAL STUDIES	(b) (4)
Exploratory endpoints of the mean change from baseline in ppFEV1 through Week 52, CF pulmonary exacerbations, and the results of the CFQ-R RD questionnaire were included because they provide data clinically meaningful to prescribers. Language was included to convey results were not statistically significant.

17 PATIENT COUNSELING INFORMATION	Updated to include information regarding the risks related to the Boxed Warning and warnings and precautions. Additional updates included edits to ensure the section was directed to patients and not prescribers consistent with the FDA guidance for industry Instructions for Use—Patient Labeling for Human Prescription Drug and Biological Products—Content and Format (July 2022).
Product Quality Sections (i.e., DOSAGE FORMS AND STRENGTHS, DESCRIPTION, HOW SUPPLIED/STORAGE AND HANDLING)	No significant changes.

L3305: ## 25. Financial Disclosure
L3306: Table 203 summarizes the financial disclosures submitted to the NDA that include Trial 102, 103, and 105. There were several significant payments that included speaker fees, steering committee and advisory board fees, and investigator-initiated grants. No investigator reported that the payments received were related to the clinical study.
L3307: The payments were unlikely to have a significant impact upon the conduct of the clinical trials, given that the majority of individual sites enrolled a small number of subjects and the pivotal efficacy trials were randomized, double-blinded, and controlled with objective spirometric, nutritional, and exacerbation related endpoints. In addition, site-based sensitivity analyses were performed on the primary endpoint and the removal of the subjects from any individual site does not affect the evaluation of efficacy or safety evaluation of this NDA.

The green horizontal line represents the median adult value. Boxplots show the statistics of the points, where the median is represented by a horizontal line, and the IQR is represented by a box. L3418: Figure 5 displays two graphs comparing chloride transport in HBE cells. The left graph shows results for F508del/F508del-HBE cells, and the right graph shows results for F508del/minimal function-HBE cells. Both graphs plot Chloride Current (\mu\text{A}/\text{cm}^2) versus Log M [VX-121]. The legend indicates five conditions: VX-121 alone, VX-121 + 18\mu\text{M} TEZ, VX-121 + 1\mu\text{M} D-IVA, VX-121 + 18\mu\text{M} TEZ + 1\mu\text{M} D-IVA, and TEZ/IVA. The data is grouped into 1 drug, 2 drugs, and 3 drugs combinations, showing increasing chloride transport with the addition of drugs.
L3419: Figure 6 displays three bar charts (A, B, and C) comparing Chloride Transport (\mu\text{A}/\text{cm}^2) in HBE cells. Panel A shows results for N1303K/N1303K HBE, Panel B for N1303K/MF HBE, and Panel C for N1303K/MF HBE. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 60 \mu\text{A}/\text{cm}^2. The X-axis shows the combination of drugs: TEZ, IVA, D-IVA, ELX, and VNZ, each with a negative control (-) and positive control (+). The data illustrates that the combination of TEZ, IVA, D-IVA, ELX, and VNZ results in the highest chloride transport, significantly higher than the combination of TEZ, IVA, D-IVA, and ELX.
L3420: Graph showing transmembrane current recorded from cells expressing the A559T CFTR variant. The current starts at Baseline. Addition of forskolin evokes chloride transport, causing a peak current. Subsequently, CFTR inhibitors are added, causing the current to return to Baseline. The graph is labeled 'Exp. A559T-ct00564502'. A scale bar indicates 2 µA over 150 sec. Red arrows indicate signals used to assess transmembrane resistance.
L3421: Figure 8 displays two scatter plots comparing chloride transport (% normal) in the presence of VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA (Y-axis) versus ELX/TEZ/IVA (X-axis) for various CFTR variants.
The left plot shows data for 74 CFTR variants included in the current efficacy supplements (NDA 212273-ES12 & NDA 217660-ES03). The right plot shows data for 23 CFTR variants that respond to Trikafta only (not to TEZ/IVA or IVA alone).
In both plots, the red diagonal line represents the scenario where VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA and ELX/TEZ/IVA elicit equal magnitude of chloride transport. Nearly all datapoints (open circles) lie above this line, indicating that VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA generally elicits greater chloride transport than ELX/TEZ/IVA for these variants. Wild-type CFTR data are shown as solid red symbols.
Data sources are noted: VNZ combo - U015 Trikafta - U032 (Left plot) and VNZ combo - U015 Trikafta - P289v4 (Right plot).
L3422: Figure 9 displays two bar charts comparing chloride transport (\mu\text{A}/\text{cm}^2) in human bronchial epithelial (HBE) cells for noncanonical splice CFTR variants, comparing treatment with ELX/TEZ/IVA (dark bars) versus VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA (light bars).
The left chart, labeled F/MF-HBE Cells, shows chloride transport values of 58.8 and 80.5 \mu\text{A}/\text{cm}^2 for ELX/TEZ/IVA and VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA, respectively.
The right chart, labeled F/F-HBE Cells, shows chloride transport values of 82.4 and 89.5 \mu\text{A}/\text{cm}^2 for ELX/TEZ/IVA and VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA, respectively.
L3423: Figure 10 shows two graphs related to CFTR variants. The left panel is a bar chart showing Mature CFTR (% Normal) for 31 variants, comparing Baseline (light gray) and VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA treatment (dark gray). The Y-axis ranges from -20 to 100. The right panel is a scatter plot showing the Change from baseline in Cl- transport (pp, normalized to normal) on the Y-axis (0 to 120) versus the Change from baseline in mature CFTR (% normal) on the X-axis (0 to 120). The data points show a lack of correlation between the two measures.

L3426: Figure 14 is a schematic diagram illustrating the study design for Study 018 (Part D). The timeline starts with a Screening period (Day -28 to Day -2), followed by Randomization. The study consists of three sequential Dosing Periods (1, 2, and 3), each separated by a 7-day Washout period. The study timeline is as follows:
Dosing Period 1: Dose 1 is administered on Day 1.
7-day Washout: Follows Dose 1.
Dosing Period 2: Dose 2 is administered on Day 8.
7-day Washout: Follows Dose 2.
Dosing Period 3: Dose 3 is administered on Day 15.
Safety Follow-up Visit: Conducted on Day 21 or 6 days after the last study drug dose.
L3427: Figure 15 is a flowchart illustrating the Study Design for Study 003. The study begins with a Screening phase, which splits into two sequences: Sequence 1 (n ~ 9) and Sequence 2 (n ~ 9).
Both sequences proceed into the Treatment Period, which is divided into two Dosing Periods:
Sequence 1: Dosing Period 1: Separate tablets of VX-121, TEZ, D-IVA, followed by Dosing Period 2: FDC tablet of VX-121/TEZ/D-IVA.
Sequence 2: Dosing Period 1: FDC tablet of VX-121/TEZ/D-IVA, followed by Dosing Period 2: Separate tablets of VX-121, TEZ, D-IVA.
A Safety Follow-up Visit is indicated 12 to 16 days after the last dose (for subjects who discontinue study drug dosing).
A timeline below the flowchart shows key dates: Day -28, Day 1, Day 14, and Day 26 (Safety Follow-up Visit for subjects who complete the Treatment Period).

Panel A is a linear plot (Y-axis: Plasma concentration in µg/mL, 0.00 to 1.00). Panel B is a semi-log plot (Y-axis: Plasma concentration in µg/mL, 0.001 to 1.000). In both panels, the left column shows results for VX-561 alone, and the right column shows results for VX-561 + Itraconazole. Observed data points are shown as filled symbols, and simulated data is shown as a solid line. The shaded region represents the 5th and 95th percentiles of the simulated data. The presence of Itraconazole generally increases the peak concentration and extends the duration of the plasma concentration profile.

L3502: Figure 95 displays six scatter plots showing the Observed and Predicted Steady-State PK Plasma Concentration-Time Profiles of D-IVA, M1-D-IVA, and M6-D-IVA in CF Subjects 6 Through 11 Years of Age Weighing <40 kg or ≥40 kg, Trial 105 (Cohort B1). L3508: Figure 101 displays two tipping point analysis heatmaps for the absolute change from baseline in ppFEV1 through Week 24 (FAS) for Trials 102 and 103. The X-axis represents the Shift parameter for VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA (0 to 90), and the Y-axis represents the Shift parameter for ELX/TEZ/IVA (0 to 90). The color scale indicates the Lower limit of 95% CI, categorized as \ge -3 (Green) or < -3 (Red).

Study 103: The heatmap shows that the Lower limit of 95% CI is \ge -3 for most combinations of shift parameters. However, for high shift parameters (VNZ/TEZ/D-IVA > 70 and ELX/TEZ/IVA > 70), the Lower limit of 95% CI is < -3 (Red).
